[DOCLING_PAGE 1]

Supplementary appendix

This appendix formed part of the original submission and has been peer reviewed. We post it as supplied by the authors.

Supplement to: Bendszus M, Fiehler J, Subtil F, et al. Endovascular thrombectomy for acute ischaemic stroke with established large infarct: multicentre, open-label, randomised trial. Lancet 2023; published online Oct 11. https://doi.org/10.1016/ S0140-6736(23)02032-9.

[DOCLING_PAGE 2]

23.04.2018

I have read this protocol and agree to conduct this clinical investigation in accordance with the design and specific provisions outlined herein. I understand the protocol, and I understand I am solely responsible to ensure the investigation is conducted in accordance with Good Clinical Practices, applicable international regulations, local regulations, and with the protocol outlined herein. I will conduct this study as outlined therein and will make reasonable effort to complete the study within the designed time flow.

I will provide copies of the protocol and all pertinent information to all individuals responsible to me who will assist in the conduct of this study. I will discuss this material with them to ensure they are fully informed regarding the endovascular treatment and the conduct of the study.

I will fulfill the requirements of my Ethics Committee (EC), and/or other oversight committee, to ensure complete and continual oversight of this clinical investigation. I will use an Informed Consent Document approved by my reviewing oversight committee(s).

I agree to report all information or data in accordance with the protocol and, in particular, I agree to report any serious adverse events as defined in this protocol to my reviewing oversight committee, where required. I agree to permit regulatory authority access to all records relating to the clinical investigation, whether paper-based or electronic data capture.

The below signature confirms I have read and understood this clinical investigational protocol and its associated amendments or attachments.

Protocol Synopsis

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=8 columns=2]
| Title | Efficacy and safety of ThrombEctomy iN Stroke with extended leSION and extended time window: a randomized, controlled trial |
| Sponsor | University of Heidelberg |
| Study Design | This study is an investigator-initiated, prospective, open label, blinded endpoint (PROBE), multinational, European, two-arm, randomized, controlled, post-market study to compare the safety and effectiveness of endovascular thrombectomy as compared to best medical care alone in the treatment of acute ischemic stroke (AIS) in patients with extended stroke lesions defined by an ASPECT score of 3-5 and in an extended time window (up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well). This is an adaptive design study with interim analyses with pre-specified stopping rules allowing for the possibility of early termination based on either a determination of study success or futility. |
| Sample Size (Adaptive) | Up to 665 subjects will be enrolled in the study and randomized for the Intention to Treat analysis set. The randomization will be stratified by time from symptom onset or last seen well (< 6h and 6-11h), stroke severity (NIHSS ≤18, NIHSS >18) Interim data analysis is planned after the primary endpoint has been obtained for one third and two thirds of the patients. At each of these sample sizes, the available 90-day mRS data for each treatment arm will be evaluated. Safety interim analysis will be performed after one third and two thirds of the patients have been included. |
| Treatment Groups | Subjects who meet the inclusion criteria will be randomized in a 1:1 ratio to one of the following two treatment arms: Arm 1: best medical care Arm 2: endovascular thrombectomy and best medical care |
| Study Objectives | The primary objective of this study is to test efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with uncertain benefit of endovascular stroke treatment, i.e. extended ischemic lesion size with an ASPECT score of 3-5 up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well as compared to best medical care alone. |

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

1 Introduction

Worldwide, stroke is the second leading cause of death, responsible for 4.4 million (9%) of the total 50.5 million deaths each year. In the EU stroke accounts for just over 500.000 deaths each year with just around one in ten men (9%) and one in eight women (12%) dying from stroke (Allender et al., 2008).

The prognosis following an acute ischemic stroke varies, depending on the stroke severity and the individual's premorbid condition, age, and post stroke complications (Adams 1999). In the United States, 20% of individuals die within the first year after a first-time stroke. The Framingham Heart Study showed that among stroke survivors, 31% needed help caring for themselves, 20% needed help when walking, and 71% had impaired vocational capacity when examined an average 7 years following the stroke.

For years, intravenous thrombolysis with recombinant tissue Plasminogen Activator (rtPA, Alteplase) represented the only effective and approved specific treatment for acute ischemic stroke and is recommended by international, European and national guidelines (ESO, 2008). Following the first positive clinical trial of thrombolysis in ischemic stroke, treatment with Alteplase was restricted to treatment initiation ≤3 hours of symptom onset but based on the results of ECASS III (Hacke et al., 2008) international guidelines now recommend intravenous thrombolysis up to 4.5 hours of symptom onset (Del Zoppo et al., 2009). Consequently, the European Medicines Agency has approved intravenous thrombolysis with Alteplase for acute stroke up to 4.5 hours of symptom onset.

In large vessel occlusions, however, recanalization after i.v. thrombolysis is infrequent and in a thrombus length of more than 8 mm almost absent (Riedel et al., 2011). Since timely recanalization is crucial for a good outcome in stroke alternative therapy is warranted in patients with large vessel occlusion. Moreover, a substantial number of patients cannot receive i.v. tPA for contraindications (e.g. anticoagulation, recent surgery, time after symptom onset beyond 4.5 hours). In these patient groups endovascular mechanical clot removal has been advocated. The first generation devices (Merci, Penumbra) achieved recanalization in up to 50%; however, clinical outcome was not substantially improved compared to historical controls (Smith et al., Stroke 2005). Safety of endovascular treatment with mechanical or pharmacological approaches was also comparable in patients under oral anticoagulants (de Marchis et al., 2011). Stent-like devices for thrombectomy also referred to as stent-retrievers have substantially improved recanalization rates and outcome compared to first generation devices (Nogueira et al., 2012, Saver et al., 2012). Recently, 5 RCTs mostly using thrombectomy by stent retrievers in the endovascular treatment of acute stroke compared to best medical care demonstrated overwhelming clinical benefit without increased adverse events (Berkhemer et al., 2015, Goyal et al., 2015, Saver et al., 2015, Campbell et al., 2015, Jovin et al, 2015). However, in these trials mostly patients with good prognostic factors (i.e. short time interval between stroke onset and endovascular treatment as well as small size of ischemic lesion prior to treatment) were included. Of 1.287 patients randomized, less than 5% were treated >6 hours of symptom onset. With regards to early ischemic signs, in four of these five trials patients with ASPECTS <6 or <7 were excluded. Only MR CLEAN did not specify pre-treatment ASPECTS values as exclusion criterion, but even in MR CLEAN only small numbers of patients with low ASPECTS values were included. In 28 patients with ASPECTS 0-4 no treatment effect was evident with an OR of 1.1 (95% CI 0.1-8.4) for benefit in the thrombectomy group, while in 92 patients with ASPECTS 5-7 with an OR of 2.0 (0.9-4.4) a clear trend for a treatment benefit was observed similar to the effect in the group of patients with ASPECTS >7 (Berkhemer et al., 2015). Thus, based on the data from these trials, insufficient evidence is available to judge whether mechanical thrombectomy is also safe and effective in patients with an extended time window or advanced signs of early ischemia, or a combination of both. This was also acknowledged by experts from the European Stroke Organisation (ESO), the European Society for Minimally Invasive Neurological Therapy (ESMINT), and the European Society of Neuroradiology (ESNR) a consensus statement on thrombectomy (Wahlgren et al, 2016). In this statement the experts clearly recommend further randomized clinical trials addressing open issues such as 'treatment in a late und unknown time windows, treating patients with imaging findings not sufficiently covered in recent trials…'. TENSION aims to close this gap and provide evidence for thrombectomy in in both late time window and patients with more extend early ischemic signs before treatment.

15

[DOCLING_PAGE 55]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

Study Objectives

The primary objective of this study is to test efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with large intracranial vessel occlusion in the anterior circulation who present with dismal prognostic factors (ASPECT score of 3-5, treatment time window of up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well (if case of unknown time of symptom onset) as compared to best medical care (i.e. iv thrombolysis if indicated, supportive care on a dedicated stroke unit) alone.

2 Study Design

2.1 General Study Design

This is an investigator-initiated, prospective, European, two-arm, randomized, controlled, open label, blinded endpoint post-market study to compare the safety and effectiveness of endovascular thrombectomy compared to best medical treatment alone in acute ischemic stroke (AIS) patients with extended stroke lesions up to 12 hours of symptom onset or last seen well. Patients who meet the inclusion criteria will be randomized to one of the following two treatment arms: best medical care alone or best medical care plus endovascular treatment with devices for endovascular thrombectomy (referred to as thrombectomy). Endpoints in this prospective open label study will be assessed blinded to the treatment assignment of the patient (PROBE design). This study will be conducted in approximately 40 sites in eight to ten European countries. This is an adaptive design study, in which there are prospectively stated interim analyses with specified stopping rules, which allow for the possibility of the study to terminate early based on either a determination of study success or of the futility to continue further enrollment.

16

[DOCLING_PAGE 56]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

Up to six hundred and sixty five (665) subjects will be enrolled and randomized in the study for the Intent to Treat (ITT) analysis set. The randomization will be stratified by time from symptom onset and stroke severity (NIHSS). The expected duration of each subject's enrollment is approximately 12 months. Subjects will be followed with assessments at 24 (+/-6) hours, hospital discharge (or 7-10 days), 90 (+/-14) days and 12 months (+/-14 days) post randomization.

A blinded core laboratory will assess baseline imaging to confirm vessel occlusion and determine ASPECT score, 24 (+/-6) hours post-randomization imaging to assess presence of ICH, and to measure core infarct volume.

2.2 Primary effectiveness endpoint

The primary effectiveness endpoint for a subject is the blinded evaluation of the ordinal mRS outcome at 90 (+/-14) days post-stroke.

2.3 Secondary Effectiveness Endpoints

The following secondary effectiveness endpoints will be described and compared between the two treatment groups:

Good outcome with percentages of subjects with mRS of 0-2 (blinded evaluation) at 90 (+/14) days post-stroke

Moderate neurological outcomes (Saver et al., 2012) at 90 days defined as percentages of subjects with 90-day mRS≤3 (blinded evaluation) at 90 (+/-14) days post-stroke

Rates of decompressive craniotomy

Core infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours post randomization based on FLAIR or T2-weighted MRI (preferred) or unenhanced CT if MRI is not feasible, as determined by a blinded core lab.

Difference of infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours post randomization from infarct volume as predicted by pre-treatment imaging.

Functional health status and quality of life 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) post stroke (EQ-5D-3L)

Post-stroke depression 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) post stroke (PHQ-4)

In patients in the thrombectomy group, recanalization measured by the TICI scale will be studied as a procedural efficacy endpoint

Functional neurological outcome at 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (simplified modified Rankin Scale questionnaire, smRSq)

In summary, the following effectiveness endpoints will be assessed at overall three time points by the indicated investigators:

17

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

2.4 Safety Endpoints

All reports of adverse events (AEs) will be classified according to System Organ Class (SOC) and Preferred Term (PT) using the MedDRA coding system. The PIs assessment of relatedness of the event to the treatment, endovascular procedure and/or disease state will be used for tabulations. The following endpoints will be presented for each treatment arm, as applicable, as part of the safety analysis:

Death or dependency 90 (±14) days after stroke (mRS 4-6)

SICH at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in SITS-MOST ('local or remote parenchymal hematoma type 2 on the imaging scan obtained 22 to 36 hours after treatment, plus neurologic deterioration, as indicated by a score on the NIHSS ≥ 4 points than the baseline value or the lowest value between baseline and 24 hours, or haemorrhage leading to death') (Wahlgren et al., 2007)

Frequencies of AEs during the first 7 days following randomization.

Frequencies of Serious AEs (SAEs) in both treatment arms at 90 (+/-14) days

Mortality rates at discharge and 90 days post-treatment

Stroke-related death (i.e., related to intracranial bleeding, midline shift, brain herniation, progression of stroke symptoms, or systemic complications from stroke such as pneumonia)

Space-occupying infarction (malignant brain edema)

New ischemic stroke

Intracranial hemorrhage will be determined by blinded core lab assessment of imaging and neurological assessment will be performed at the study site by independent neurologist certified in NIHSS, adjudication of SICH will be done by an outcome adjudication committee).

2.5 Angiographic Endpoints

The following angiographic endpoints will be assessed in the thrombectomy arm only:

Rate of embolization, either distal to the target occlusion or to previously unaffected territories (determined by independent core lab)

Rate of successful restoration of flow of the occluded target arterial vessel, defined as TICI 2b or better (determined by independent core lab)

18

[DOCLING_PAGE 58]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

2.6 Imaging Endpoints

Infarct volume at follow-up after 24 (±6) hours will be measured by MRI or CT and compared between the endovascular thrombectomy arm and the best medical care arm, the variable being transformed if necessary.

Difference from predicted infarct volume will be calculated based on CT perfusion or MR perfusion imaging acquired pre randomization (Forkert, ND et al. 2013).

3 Eligibility of Subjects and Exclusions

Patients eligible for initial study screening are those who present with symptoms attributable to acute thrombotic occlusion in the M1 segment of the MCA and/or distal ICA treatable within 12 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well. All such patients who meet all general eligibility criteria will be randomized following informed consent procedures approved by the institution's Ethics Committee (EC).

3.1 General Inclusion Criteria

Prior to enrollment in this study, candidates for randomization must meet ALL of the following criteria:

Patient is in facility with resources available (i.e. cath lab, INT) to ensure endovascular treatment within 90 minutes from inclusion imaging to groin puncture, if patient were to be randomized to thrombectomy arm.

Clinical Inclusion criteria

Randomization within 11 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well.

Endovascular treatment is expected to be finished within 12 hours after known symptom onset or last seen well by judgment of the interventional neuroradiologist in charge

Patient must demonstrate clinical signs and symptoms attributable to target area of occlusion consistent with the diagnosis of ischemic stroke, including impairment of the following: language, motor function, sensation, cognition, gaze, and/or vision for at least 30 minutes without relevant improvement.

Female and male patient above 18 up to 80 years of age

Moderate to severe stroke (NIHSS Score of <26)

Signed informed consent (IC) form by patient or legal guardian, or inclusion of patient's presumptive will by investigator under emergency situation, but after consultation of an independent physician who is familiar with these types of illness if the other options are not possible according to local approval.

Prior to new focal neurological deficit, mRS score was ≤2.

19

[DOCLING_PAGE 59]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

Imaging Inclusion Criteria

A new focal occlusion confirmed by imaging (MRA/CTA) to be accessible to the thrombectomy device, and located in the M1 of the middle cerebral artery (MCA) and/or the intracranial segment of the distal internal carotid artery (ICA).

CT (non-contrast CT) or DWI with an ASPECT score of 3-5

3.2 General Exclusion Criteria

Candidates must not be randomized if ANY of the following criteria are met:

Clinical exclusion criteria

Patient is an active participant in another drug or device treatment trial for any disease state or patient is expected to start participation in another drug or device treatment trial while enrolled in this protocol, unless approved by Sponsor.

Patient has pre-existing neurological or psychiatric disease that could impede the study results or would confound the neurological or functional evaluations.

Patient has history of contraindication for contrast medium.

Patient is known to have infective endocarditis

Patient's anticipated life expectancy is less than 12 months

Imaging exclusion criteria

Extracranial CT or MR angiography showing high grade stenosis expected to require acute stent placement (e.g. ICA stenting) during the procedure

CT or MRI with evidence of mass effect or intracranial tumor

If CT perfusion is done per institutional standard: CBV drop on CTP with an ASPECT score of 0-2

3.3 Angiographic Screening Criteria (thrombectomy arm only)

Subjects will be considered to have failed angiographic screening if ANY of the following conditions apply on DSA images:

Excessive arterial tortuosity or other anatomical impediment proximal to the intracranial occlusion that prevents probing the target occlusion.

No occlusion is evident during baseline angiography

A subject in the thrombectomy treatment arm who fails the Angiographic Screening Criteria will be:

deemed to be an angiographic screen failure (but not a protocol deviation)

followed in the study through the 12 months follow-up

included in the ITT primary endpoint mRS shift analysis; the actual 90 day mRS assessment will be utilized

included in the ITT secondary endpoint success/failure responder analysis; the success/fail outcome will be determined by the actual 90 day mRS assessment

20

[DOCLING_PAGE 60]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

Notes: 1) Subjects randomized to the thrombectomy arm that show rapidly improving neurological signs prior to or at the time of endovascular treatment should not undergo the endovascular procedure, but will remain in the ITT analysis set. 2) If no occlusion is detected angiographically, clot retrieval should not be attempted, and subject will remain in the ITT analysis set.

4 Study Assessments

4.1 Screening

All assessments in the study are made on basis of good standard of care and not specifically for the study. Besides the assessments at the different time points specified in the protocol other patient data will be recorded systematically outside of the protocol (e.g. blood pressure, heart beat rate) since all patients will stay on certified stroke units where systematic collection of these data is prerequisite for approval. There are no follow-up requirements for subjects who are general eligibility screen failures.

4.2 Randomization and Baseline

Subjects who meet all the inclusion criteria's will be enrolled, randomized and assigned a five digit identification number (subject study number). The first digit assigned represents the country; the next two digits represent the site identification, followed by a hyphen and two subsequent digits which represent the sequential enrollment number. For example, ID: 301-05 would represent the fifth subject enrolled at site '01' of country '3'.

Randomization will be done via the eCRF. Subjects will be randomized to one of the following treatment arms: 'best medical care' or 'best medical care plus thrombectomy'

A randomization schedule (1:1 ratio, best medical care alone: best medical care plus thrombectomy) stratified by stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18), time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 6-11 hours) will be generated as described in Section 8.

To randomize the subject, an authorized member of the study site staff will access the webbased system with their individual account information, and will, at a minimum, input the subject identification number, NIHSS, and time from symptom onset. The site will then be provided with a treatment assignment for the subject. Only one subject may be randomized at a time. Randomization assignments will be documented.

4.2.1 Baseline Imaging

All subjects will have baseline imaging (MRI including DWI or CT to rule out ICH and exclude major infarction, and MRA/CTA of neck and head to confirm an occlusion in the M1 segment of MCA and/or intracranial ICA and to rule out carotid dissection, high grade stenosis ≥70%, or excessive tortuosity proximal to occlusion) performed as part of institutional standard of care procedures for acute stroke management. All imaging data of this baseline imaging (including raw data) must be provided to the Core Lab for blinded assessment of flow occlusion, location of the occlusion and determination of ASPECT score.

21

[DOCLING_PAGE 61]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

4.2.2 Baseline Visit

At admission a physical exam, as well as clinical and neurological assessments will be completed per institutional standard of care. Data to be collected and then captured on eCRF include:

Relevant laboratory test results, including hemoglobin, hematocrit, white and red blood cell count (WBC and RBC), creatinine, international normalized ration (INR), partial thromboplastin time (PTT), and pregnancy test results for women of child-bearing potential

Vital signs (blood pressure, heart beat rate)

Relevant concomitant medications administered (antithrombotic [including anti-platelet, anticoagulant, and fibrinolytics], for treatment of diabetes mellitus, atrial fibrillation, hypertension, vascular disease), from 7 days prior to date of admission through start of the study treatment

Significant findings from clinical assessment and physical exam

All significant neurological findings, pre-ictus mRS, and NIHSS assessed by qualified personnel at time of admission.

4.3 Study Treatment

4.3.1 Best medical treatment

Best medical treatment will be performed as detailed in established Standard Operating Procedures, following regional guidelines (AHA, ESO, DSG, local country, etc.). If applicable, the reason for iv tPA ineligibility has to be documented on the eCRF.

4.3.2 Thrombectomy (Cerebral Blood Flow Restoration Procedure)

If the subject is randomized to the thrombectomy arm, then the steps for the endovascular procedure should be subsequently completed; groin puncture should be no later than 90 minutes from inclusion imaging. If a subject randomized to thrombectomy fails the angiographic screening, endovascular procedure should proceed as clinically recommended, and subject will remain in ITT population. If a subject randomized to thrombectomy arm shows rapidly improving neurologic symptoms prior to procedure, endovascular procedure should not proceed, but subject will remain in ITT population.

All subjects randomly assigned to the thrombectomy arm, except those with rapidly improving neurologic symptoms or no angiographic evidence of occlusion, will be treated with endovascular thrombectomy, and will have the procedural imaging (pre-, intra-, post-procedure) for site evaluation of TICI flow at the start and end of the procedure. In the thrombectomy arm, the start of the procedure is the time the femoral artery is accessed (groin puncture), and it must occur no later than 90 minutes from MRA/CTA confirmation of the occlusion. After femoral access is achieved, subjects will be administered IV heparin per institutional protocol, but it is recommended not exceed 2000 unit bolus and maximum 500 units per hour. A microcatheter introduced over an appropriately sized guidewire can then be manipulated across the occlusive thrombus under high magnification roadmap fluoroscopic control. After removal of the microwire, angiographic runs should be performed per standard procedure to determine the length of the target occlusion. Site-assessed TICI scores and procedural adverse events (if any) will be recorded on source documents for study.

22

[DOCLING_PAGE 62]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

Approved devices for endovascular thrombectomy may be used within the trial according to the local investigators choice. Devices for thrombectomy will be used per IFU. In the thrombectomy arm, the end of the procedure is the time the guiding catheter is removed from the subject. Complete angiogram runs should show the occlusion/flow prior to crossing the occlusion and post device removal.

Post-procedure, the arterial access will be secured with a closure device or suture. Subjects will be asked to lie flat with a pressure bandage over the groin or radial puncture site according to the local standard of care.

Data to be collected during procedure and then captured on eCRF include

Use of general anesthesia with intubation (GA) or conscious sedation (CS) with local anesthesia at start of procedure and at time of removal of guide catheter

Procedure times (groin puncture time, time of microcatheter placement across the occlusion, time of initial flow restoration, time(s) of thrombectomy device removal, time guide catheter is removed)

Accessory and adjunctive devices used (guidecatheter, guidewire, intermediate catheter, microcatheter)

Site-assessed TICI score: pre-procedure and post-procedure

Number and type of devices for thrombectomy

Rescue procedures with medications besides thrombectomy

Intraprocedural complications

Presence of vasospasm

Evidence of clot migration or embolization

Dissections/perforations

Device deficiencies, malfunction, complaints

4.3.3 Treatment Medications

All treatment medications, regardless of study arm, will be recorded on the source document and eCRF.

23

[DOCLING_PAGE 63]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

4.3.4 Post Treatment (Through Hospital Discharge)

Brain imaging performed at 24 (+/-6) hours post-treatment according to clinical practice will be collected and centrally analyzed by the image core lab to assess for intracranial hemorrhage and for infarct volume assessment.

A physical exam, as well as clinical and neurological assessments, will be completed per institutional standard of care at 24 (+/-6) hours post treatment, and at hospital discharge (or 710 days, whichever comes earlier). Data to be collected and then captured on eCRFs include:

Vital signs (blood pressure and heart rate).

Relevant concomitant medications (including anti-platelet, anticoagulant, and fibrinolytics).

Significant findings from clinical assessment and physical exam (i.e. all new, worsening, or improved conditions).

All significant neurological findings, NIHSS Score (24 (+/-6) hrs and discharge), mRS (discharge only), collected during independent neurological assessment by qualified personnel.

4.4 Follow-up Visits

Follow-up visits will be performed at 90 days and 12 months after the hospital admission date. The window for the 90-day follow-up visit is 90 ± 14 days which is from 76 days to 104 days after the hospital admission date. The window for the 12 month follow-up visit is 12 month ± 14 days which is from 351 days to 379 days after the hospital admission date.

4.4.1 Follow-up Visit at Day 90

The Day 90 follow-up evaluations should be performed at the investigational site. All subjects entered into the study will undergo a neurological assessment per institutional standard of care. Data to be collected and then captured on eCRFs include:

Significant findings from clinical assessment and physical exam (i.e. all new, worsening, or improved conditions since discharge)

NIHSS Score and mRS collected during independent neurological assessment by qualified, certified personnel blinded to the treatment.

Patient-reported functional health status and quality of life using the Patient Reported Outcomes Measurement Information System 10 (PROMIS-10) and EQ-5D-3L

Assessment of depressive symptom by self-report using the Patient Health Questionnaire for Depression and Anxiety (PHQ-4)

Structured interview questionnaires to collect data concerning use of outpatient and informal care

24

[DOCLING_PAGE 64]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

Only in exceptional cases (e.g. patient is not willing or able to come the investigational site), mRS assessment can be performed via telephone interview by a Neurologist not involved in the study and treatment of this patient.

4.4.2 Follow-up Visit at 12 Months

The twelve month follow-up evaluations will be performed by telephone interview. Data to be collected and then captured on eCRFs include:

Functional outcome assessed by qualified, certified personnel blinded to the treatment using the simplified modified Rankin Scale questionnaire (smRSq)

Patient-reported functional health status and quality of life using the Patient Reported Outcomes Measurement Information System 10 (PROMIS-10) and EQ-5D-3L

Assessment of depressive symptom by self-report using the Patient Health Questionnaire for Depression and Anxiety (PHQ-4)

Structured interview questionnaires to collect data concerning use of outpatient and informal care

Collection of data on hospitalizations during the study

4.4.3 Unscheduled Follow-up Visit

If an unscheduled follow-up visit occurs post-treatment at the investigational site during the study, incidence of any new or unresolved adverse events will be assessed. If the visit is due to a change in neurological status, NIHSS and mRS will be completed by a certified rater.

If the visit is due to a change in neurological status, any imaging, diagnostic test results or interventions performed must be documented.

4.5 Study Completion (End of Study)

The subject will complete study participation when the subject has been assessed at the 12 Months visit or when an early withdrawal is documented for the participant.

Possible reasons for premature discontinuation may include, but are not limited to, the following:

Subject decides to withdraw from the study

Lost to follow-up: after three documented attempts to reach the subject by telephone have failed, a certified letter will be sent to the subject. The subject will be considered lost to follow-up if this communication is unsuccessful.

Death

A subject or his/her legal authorized representative can stop the subject's participation in this study by withdrawing consent , or decide to discontinue participation if subject is no longer able to participate in the study (owing to adverse events, concomitant disease or administrative reasons). If a subject or his/her legal authorized representative decides to withdraw subject from the study, it can be done at any time. However, the reason for discontinuation must be recorded in the source documentation and on the eCRF.

25

[DOCLING_PAGE 65]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

Also, the investigator can decide on the study termination of subject in cases of AEs which make the continuation not desirable, major protocol deviations, or non-compliance of the subject.

Withdrawing consent will not result in any penalty whatsoever for the subject. Further treatment will be performed according to local practice. In case of a procedural-related adverse event that occurred as a result of participating in this study, the subject will be treated according to local practice

The data of the subject who withdraws his/her informed consent, collected prior to withdrawal, will not be discarded, and will be used in the final analysis of the study. However, if the subject or his/her legal representative explicitly asks for data removal, his/her data will be destroyed and not be integrated in the ITT analysis. Subjects who discontinue study participation prematurely will not be replaced.

Subjects with unresolved adverse events at the time of withdrawal must be followed until the adverse experience resolves, a stable or chronic clinical end-point is reached, or death occurs.

5 Imaging Core Laboratory

For all study subjects, a core laboratory will perform the qualitative and semi-quantitative assessments for vessel occlusion and ASPECT Score at baseline as well as ICH and infarct volume assessment at 24 (+/-6) hours. These assessments will be performed for both treatment arms by readers who are blinded to the treatment received. Imaging needs to be performed according to the study Core Lab Manuals. Furthermore, the core lab will assess the angiograms for initial site, degree (TICI score) and length of occlusion and post-treatment degree of revascularization (TICI score). All sites must provide imaging that is suitable for analysis, and all images must be de-identified prior to submission. The blinding to the therapy arm will be guaranteed by separate reading of angiography and CTA/MRA.

6 Statistical Methods

The following sections provide a general description of the statistical plan for the analysis of study data. A separate Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) document that provides greater detail of data derivations and analyses to be performed will be completed within one year of first subject enrollment and prior to the first interim analysis, which is planned when one third of the patients have completed the 3-months follow-up. The SAP will reflect the protocol and any amendments implemented at the time the SAP is finalized. Amendments to the SAP will be possible any time before the database is locked. Any deviations from the final SAP will be noted in the clinical study report.

26

[DOCLING_PAGE 66]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

6.1 Study Design

The general study design is described in Section 3.1 above.

6.2 Treatment Assignment

Subjects will be randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive best medical treatment or best medical treatment plus thrombectomy. Block randomization will be used, stratified by stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and >18), and time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 6-11h of wake up stroke). Block lengths may vary, and details of the procedure are withheld from study personnel to retain allocation concealment as much as possible in this type of design.

6.3 Subject Analysis Sets

The following have been defined for analysis: an Intent-to-Treat (ITT) analysis set, a Safety analysis set, and a Per Protocol (PP) analysis set. The primary endpoint shift analysis and all secondary endpoint efficacy analyses will be carried out in the ITT analysis set. Missing data will be imputed for the primary analysis. Supportive analyses of these endpoints will be carried out on the PP analysis set. Interim analyses for early stopping are based on available data in the Intent-to-Treat analysis set.

The analysis of the safety endpoints will be carried out on the Safety analysis set.

6.3.1 ITT Analysis Set

The ITT analysis set consists of all randomized subjects enrolled into the study. Subjects will be analyzed according to the treatment to which they were randomized. A sensitivity analysis will be performed to estimate the effect of missing data. For the primary endpoint multiple imputations in a model including strong prognostic variables (to be detailed in the SAP) will be used.

6.3.2 Safety Analysis Set

The Safety analysis set consists of all randomized subjects enrolled in the study. For the thrombectomy arm all subjects undergoing groin puncture independent of randomization will be included. For the control group all other subjects (not undergoing groin puncture) will be included.

6.3.3 PP Analysis Set

The PP analysis set includes all subjects who meet the following criteria:

the subject was treated with the treatment to which they were randomized, and

there are no clinically meaningful deviations from the protocol and/or general eligibility criteria,

the 90-day assessment of mRS is available.

27

[DOCLING_PAGE 67]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

6.3.4 Dataset Flow Diagram

A detailed account of all consented and randomized subjects will be presented in a dataset flow diagram to display the analysis datasets, beginning with the ITT analysis set and explicitly listing the subjects with distinguishing reasons for exclusion from the other datasets. A template of this dataset flow diagram is given in Figure 2.

6.4 Sample Size

Simulations were carried out using an assumption for the possible true distribution of mRS from the literature (Goyal et al 2015, Supplement: distribution of mRS in patients with an ASPECT score of 7 or less (5)) and a proportional odds alternative with an odds ratio of 1.5 to be assessed using the primary endpoint mRS shift analysis. Outcome profiles were assumed for the treatment arm, as in Table 2 (for each row, table entries are the assumed proportion of subjects in the patient population who would have the indicated mRS outcome if treated with the control or thrombectomy, respectively).

Table 2 Scenarios used for sample size planning.

mRS Outcome

28

[DOCLING_PAGE 68]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=3 columns=7]
|  | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5&6 |
| Control | 0.02 | 0.06 | 0.12 | 0.16 | 0.24 | 0.40 |
| Thrombectomy | 0.03 | 0.09 | 0.16 | 0.18 | 0.23 | 0.31 |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

Under the assumption of these distributions, a total of 620 patients is required to achieve a power of 80% for a one-sided test at the 0.025 level.

The trial will undergo two interim analyses with possible premature stopping for futility or early success. For details, see section 6.5.6. As a consequence, the total sample size needs to be increased. With a power cumulative error spending function of parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for the alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility), up to a maximum of 633 patients will be required if the trial does not stop early.

Assuming a 5% drop-out rate of patients to assess the primary endpoint obtained 3 months after inclusion an effective sample size of 665 should be planned to be recruited.

6.5 Analysis Plan

The following sections provide a general description of the statistical plan for the analysis of study data. An extensive version of the analysis plan will be finalized before the trial database is locked.

Summary tables for subject demographics and baseline characteristics will be provided and comparisons will be made between study arms for the ITT and PP analysis sets. Procedural characteristics unique to usage of devices for thrombectomy will be described for subjects in the best medical care plus thrombectomy treatment group (within the ITT analysis set). The primary endpoint will be summarized and compared between study groups for the ITT and PP analysis sets. Secondary effectiveness endpoints will be summarized and compared between study groups for the ITT analysis set. Safety endpoints will be summarized and compared between study groups for the Safety analysis set.

In general, summaries will be presented by treatment group pooled across occlusion location and investigators/sites, and by occlusion location within treatment group in the relevant analysis populations. Descriptive statistics for dichotomous/categorical variables will include number and percent of subjects in each category (including missing), by treatment group. Descriptive statistics for continuous variables will include number of non-missing and missing subjects, minimum, lower quartile, median, upper quartile, maximum, mean, and standard deviation, stratified by treatment group. Regarding comparisons between treatment groups, Chi-squared test (Fisher's exact test where appropriate) will be utilized for the comparison of categorical variables and the t-test (or the Mann-Whitney U test when appropriate) will be utilized for the comparison of continuous variables. Data for the primary endpoint will be presented by treatment group (pooled over all sites) and by treatment group within study site . Aside from protocol specified hypothesis tests, confidence intervals will be presented to facilitate clinical judgment of the secondary safety and effectiveness endpoints, and not to test hypotheses.

29

[DOCLING_PAGE 69]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

Confidence intervals for dichotomous or ordinal endpoints will be reported on the odds ratio scale.

6.5.1 Subject Disposition

All subjects who are consented and given a subject identification number will be accounted for in Subject Disposition, overall and by occlusion location and study site. Numbers and percentages of subjects who are consented, randomized, receive treatment, are designated Angiographic Screen Failures (only best medical treatment plus thrombectomy arm), complete the study per protocol or withdraw from the study prematurely along with the reason for discontinuation, and who comprise the analysis sets will be presented. The number of subjects who were seen at each specified study visit will also be tabulated. A subject data listing, sorted by subject identification number within site, will be provided. The listing will include date of consent, date of treatment, date of study completion or early termination along with reason for early termination and indications of membership in the various analysis populations. The number and type of protocol deviations will be summarized.

6.5.2 Screening/Baseline Characteristics

Demographics, including gender and age will be listed in a patient data listing and tabulated for each analysis set. Baseline disease characteristics including historic (pre-ictus) mRS, presenting symptom(s) and results of pre-treatment imaging with a description of the occlusion location will also be summarized.

6.5.3 Thrombectomy Procedure Characteristics

In addition, for subjects in the thrombectomy ITT analysis set in whom treatment with devices for thrombectomy is attempted; the following procedure characteristics will also be described:

Time from symptom onset to groin puncture

Time from 'door' (presentation in ER) to groin puncture

Time from MRA/CTA confirmation of occlusion to groin puncture

Time from groin puncture to first microcatheter placement across the occlusion

Treatment duration from time the femoral artery is accessed until the time the guiding catheter is removed from the subject, Number of devices for thrombectomy used

Type of rescue procedures

Fluoroscopy time

6.5.4 Primary Effectiveness Endpoint Analysis

The primary effectiveness endpoint analysis (referred to as a 'mRS shift' analysis) is carried out as a parameter test in the ordinal logistic regression model with the mRS ordinal scale as response variable and treatment group, stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18), time from symptom onset until randomization (0-6 hours and 6-11hours) as explanatory variables. For this mRS shift analysis, ordinal mRS outcomes in categories 5 and 6 are pooled into one category 5. The null and alternative hypotheses for this primary endpoint are H0: β=0 and HA:

30

[DOCLING_PAGE 70]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

β≠0, where β is the parameter for the treatment effect in the logistic model. This model is based on the assumption of proportional odds, with constant underlying treatment effect (odds ratio) at any level of the MRS scale. The treatment effect will be tested using the likelihood ratio test of the treatment parameter. In a secondary analysis, the primary analysis will be repeated for the per protocol population.

6.5.5 Secondary Effectiveness Endpoints

Good and moderate neurological outcome defined as 90-day mRS≤2 and 3, respectively, will be analyzed by a logistic model with the same explanatory variables as for the primary endpoint. The responder rates will be analyzed using a likelihood-ratio test of the treatment parameter, where the null hypothesis will be rejected and the alternative hypothesis will be concluded if the two-sided p-value for the null hypothesis is less than 0.05, with the responder rates being higher for the treatment group.

The following other secondary effectiveness endpoints will also be compared between the two treatment groups:

Rates of decompressive craniotomy performed for space-occupying supratentorial infarction. This will also be analyzed with help of a logistic model with the same explanatory variables as for the primary endpoint.

Core infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours post-treatment based on FLAIR or T2-weighted MRI (preferred) or unenhanced CT if MRI is not feasible, as determined by a blinded core lab. Treatment effect with respect to this endpoint will be estimated using a log-linear model stratified according to the volumetric method. Patients with decompressive craniotomy within 30 hours will have their highest pre-surgery value substituted for the 30 hour value.

Difference from predicted infarct volume will be calculated based on CT perfusion or MR perfusion imaging acquired pre randomization. Details will be specified in an imaging manual. Effects with respect to treatment will be calculated and tested using a t-test (or a Mann-Whitney U test if the distribution is not Gaussian).

Functional health status and quality of life 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PROMIS-10, EQ-5D-3L). Treatment effect with respect to this endpoint will be elicited using a Mann-Whitney U test and a t-test on the VAS result (EQ-5D).

Differences in scores of post-stroke depression (PHQ-4) 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke will be studied using a Mann-Whitney U test

6.5.5.1 Safety Endpoints

Safety endpoints are defined in Section 3.4 above. The reporting of Adverse Events is more thoroughly described in Section 7.1 below. Although the study was not powered to test hypotheses for these secondary safety endpoints, two-sided p-values (equal vs. not equal) will be presented to facilitate clinical interpretation.

All reports of adverse events (AEs) will be classified according to System Organ Class (SOC) and Preferred Term (PT) using the MedDRA coding system. The PIs assessment of relatedness of the event to the treatment device, procedure and/or disease state will be used for tabulations.

31

[DOCLING_PAGE 71]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

The following endpoints will be presented for each treatment arm, as applicable, as part of the safety analysis:

SICH at 24 (1+/-6) hours according to the three definitions given in section 2.4 will be compared between treatment groups using logistic regression.

Parenchymal hemorrhage type 2 (PH-2). Neurological deterioration defined as an increase in NIHSS score by 4 or more points from baseline. This will be modeled using logistic regression.

Frequencies of AEs during the first 7 days as well as serious AEs (SAEs) in both treatment arms over the whole study participation. These will be tabulated by MedDRA System Organ Class and relatedness and by MedDRA System Organ Class and severity, and by System Organ Class and Preferred Term using number of events and number of patients with events.

Overall survival and stroke-related death (where deaths from other causes will be interpreted as a censoring event) will be modeled using Cox proportional hazard regression.

Space-occupying infarction (malignant brain edema)

New ischemic stroke

6.5.5.2 Angiographic Endpoints

Angiographic endpoints are defined in Section 3.5 above. They will be tabulated using the methods described in section 8.5.

o 3

TENSION

6.5.6 Interim Analysis

After one third and two thirds of patients have completed the study, respectively, interim analyses take place to stop the trial for early success if a one-sided test suggests a treatment effect or for futility if the same test suggests that a rejection of the null hypothesis at the final analysis would be highly unlikely. The interim analysis pattern to be used will employ a power cumulative error spending function (Jennison, C. and Turnbull, B. W. (2000) Group Sequential Methods with Applications to Clinical Trials, New York: Chapman & Hall), with a parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility). The corresponding stopping boundaries (Z-values) at the 2 interim analyses and the final analysis are equal to 3.11, 2.46 and 2.01 for the alpha spending function (efficacy), and -2.38, -0.11 and 2.01 for the beta-pending function (futility). The corresponding cumulative stopping probabilities under the null hypothesis (CRef=0) and alternative hypothesis (CRef=1) are given in the table below;

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=9 columns=6]
| Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) | Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) | Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) | Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) | Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) | Expected Cumulative Stopping Probabilities Reference = CRef * (Alt Reference) |
| CRef | Expected Stopping Stage | Source | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities |
| CRef | Expected Stopping Stage | Source | Stage_1 | Stage_2 | Stage_3 |
| 0.0000 | 2.526 | Reject Null | 0.00093 | 0.00741 | 0.02500 |
| 0.0000 | 2.526 | Accept Null | 0.00876 | 0.45706 | 0.97500 |
| 0.0000 | 2.526 | Total | 0.00969 | 0.46447 | 1.00000 |
| 1.0000 | 2.479 | Reject Null | 0.06952 | 0.44348 | 0.80000 |
| 1.0000 | 2.479 | Accept Null | 0.00003 | 0.00780 | 0.20000 |
| 1.0000 | 2.479 | Total | 0.06955 | 0.45128 | 1.00000 |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

The test statistic generated from the ordinal logistic model is the t-statistic for the treatment parameter, calculated by dividing the parameter estimate by its standard error (Dements, Land 1994).

After data on one third or two thirds of the total number of patients have been collected, an interim analysis will take place. If the t-statistic based on the current number of patients is crosses the pre-specified limits, the DSMB may recommend suspending the trial for early success or futility.

Summary safety reports will be generated and provided to the DSMB for review at pre-specified time intervals, but at least three times over the course of the study, where the first and second time may not be after the first and second interim analysis, respectively. Details of the DSMB reports and review processes will be defined in the DSMB charter.

33

[DOCLING_PAGE 73]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

6.6 Missing Data

Outlier values will be evaluated for their validity; all data will be included unless judged to be invalid (e.g., deemed incompatible with life by Medical Monitor and/or deemed invalid by the DSMB).

Efforts will be undertaken at study sites to reduce the amount of missing data. Due to the severity of the condition and the short 90 day follow-up period, very little loss to follow-up is anticipated. For the primary effectiveness endpoint, multiple imputations in a model including strong prognostic variables (to be detailed in the SAP) will be used. Also, sensitivity analyses will be undertaken to explore the effect of missing data on the endpoint and test the robustness of the estimate. The methods for handling missing data will be described further in the SAP.

7 Safety and Adverse Events

7.1 Adverse Events and Serious Adverse Events: Definitions

An Adverse Event (AE) is any untoward medical occurrence in a study subject, user or other person, and which does not necessarily have a causal relationship with the product or treatment; any unfavorable and unintended sign, symptom, injury, or disease temporally associated with the treatment. A preexisting condition should not be reported as an adverse event unless the condition worsens or episodes increase in frequency during the adverse event reporting period.

A Serious Adverse Event (SAE) includes any of the following events that may or may not be considered related to the treatment according to the GCP and ISO 14155;

Led to a death,

Led to a serious deterioration in health that resulted in:

a life-threatening illness or injury, or

a permanent impairment of a body structure or a body function, or

patient hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization, or medical or surgical intervention to prevent life threatening illness or injury or permanent impairment to a body structure or a body function.

medical or surgical intervention to prevent life-threatening illness or injury or permanent impairment to a body structure or a body function,

Led to fetal distress, fetal death or a congenital abnormality or birth defect.

Otherwise medically relevant

34

[DOCLING_PAGE 74]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

7.2 Documentation of Adverse Events

Each subject in the study will be questioned about adverse events (AE) on the day of hospital discharge (or 7 days, whichever occurs first). Any time during the clinical investigation, the subject may volunteer information that resembles an AE. The investigator will record the nature, severity, treatment and outcome of the AE, and will determine the relationship to device, study-specific treatment or underlying disease.

The severity of an AE refers to the extent of which the patient is impaired in his daily routine. The following categories of adverse event severity are to be used:

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=5 columns=2]
| Grade 1 | Mild AE Signs and symptoms which can be easily tolerated. Symptoms can be ignored or disappear when the subject is distracted. |
| Grade 2 | Moderate AE: Symptoms cause discomfort but are tolerable, they cannot be ignored and affect normal activity. |
| Grade 3 | Severe AE: Symptoms strongly affect normal activity. |
| Grade 4 | Life-threatening or disabling AE |
| Grade 5 | Death related to AE |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

All AEs grade 3 to 5 will be documented in the site´s source documents and subject to eCRF documentation (whether or not related to the IMP). AEs grade 1 or 2 do not have to be documented in the eCRF. All required treatments and outcomes of theses AEs must be recorded as well.

All documented AEs should be followed until they are resolved, become stable or chronic, or subject's participation in the study ends. If at the time of the 90-day follow-up visit there is an AE which started during the study, but has not yet resolved, the investigator should make all reasonable efforts to determine its eventual outcome afterwards.

Adverse Events (AE) may or may not be related to the study-specific treatment. An Investigator must make the assessment of relationship to the treatment for each AE. The Investigator should decide whether, in his or her medical judgment, there is a reasonable possibility that the event may have been caused by the study-specific treatment. To evaluate the causality of an AE in regards to the study treatment following consideration should be applied:

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=2 columns=2]
| Related (reasonable possibility) | The temporal relationship between the event and the treatment is compelling and/or follows a known or suspected response pattern to the study-specific treatment, and the event cannot be explained by subject's medical condition, other therapies, or accident. |
| Not related | The event can be readily explained by other factors such as the subject's underlying medical condition, concomitant therapy, or accident and no plausible temporal or biologic relationship exists between the study-specific treatment and the event |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

The outcome of an AE will be classified as follows:

35

[DOCLING_PAGE 75]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018

TENSION

Recovered/resolved:

All signs and symptoms of an AE disappeared without any sequels at the time of the last interrogation.

Not recovered/not resolved:

Signs and symptoms of an AE are mostly unchanged or worsened at the time of the last interrogation.

Recovered/resolved with sequel:

Actual signs and symptoms of an AE disappeared but there are sequels related to the AE.

Fatal:

Resulting in death. If there is more than one AE only the AE leading to death (possibly related) will be characterized as 'fatal'.

Unknown:

The outcome is unknown or implausible and the information cannot be supplemented or verified.

7.3 Anticipated Adverse Events

Anticipated AE are events the nature of which is consistent with the safety information contained in device documentation, i.e. in the Instructions for Use, IFU of the medical device used for the study-specific procedures. 'Anticipated' definitely does not mean that the event may be expected to occur in the course of the underlying disease .

The assessment if an event is anticipated or not will be carried out by the investigator as well as be the second assessor.

The following list of adverse events/complications (derived from IFUs) must be taken into account during assessment of 'anticipatedness'. Only events listed below are anticipated :

Hematoma and haemorrhage at puncture site

Perforation or dissection of the vessel

Vasoconstriction (Vasospasm)

Change in mental status

Persistent neurological deficits

Neurologic deterioration including stroke progression and stroke in new vascular territory

Brain edema

Ischemia

Infection

Allergic reactions

Air embolism

Intracerebral/intracranial haemorrhage

Vascular occlusion

Pseudo aneurysm formation

Aneurysm perforation

Post procedure bleeding

Distal embolization including to a previous uninvolved territory

Adverse reaction/anaphylaxis to antiplatelet/anticoagulation agents

Device(s) deformation, collapse, fracture or malfunction

7.4 (Unanticipated) Serious Adverse Device Effects

SAEs which are related to the medical device are referred to as Serious Adverse Device Effects ( SADE ).

SADE which are unanticipated are referred to as Unanticipated Serious Adverse Device Effects ( USADE ).

7.5 Reporting of Serious Adverse Events by the Investigator

All SAEs occurring between randomization and hospital discharge have to be reported to the PV-department at KKS .

The timeline of reporting depends on two characteristics of SAEs, i.e. on causality and on ' anticipatedness '.

USADEs (Unanticipated Serious Adverse Effects), i.e. those SAEs which are both unanticipated and related to the study-specific treatment will be reported immediately but at the latest within 24 hours of investigator's first awareness about the event.

All other SAEs will be reported at the latest within 7 days of investigator's first awareness about the event.

Reporting will be carried out by faxing the SAE-form to the PV-department at KKS Heidelberg:

Fax-number: 0049 (0)6221- 5633725

The report needs to be as complete as possible and contain information regarding: identification of the clinical trial anonymized subject identification, description of the event (start date, intensity, assessment of the causal relationship and 'anticipatedness', treatment of the SAE and outcome). The reports have to be dated and signed by the investigator.

The PV-department at KKS Heidelberg is responsible for registering all incoming SAE, checking on their completeness, general correctness, and plausibility.

The following events are not considered SAEs and therefore need not to be reported to the PV-department of KKS Heidelberg:

37

[DOCLING_PAGE 77]

Protocol Version V4.1 from 04.06.2018 TENSION

Hospitalization due to diagnostic or elective surgical procedures for pre-existing condition, unless the condition becomes more severe or increases in frequency

Hospitalization for therapy of the study target disease explicitly anticipated and defined in the protocol

7.6 Second Assessment

All SAEs will be subject to a second assessment.

The second assessment includes re-evaluation of causality and 'anticipatedness', as well as appraisal of the influence of a given SAE on the benefit-risk assessment of the trial.

The second assessment will be performed by (a) designated person(s), who will be independent from the reporting investigator.

The second assessor will fill out a 'Second Assessment Sheet' for each SAE and send it back per fax to the PV-department at the KKS Heidelberg within 48 hours:

Fax-number: 0049 (0)6221- 5633725

7.7 Expedited Reporting by the Sponsor

All SAEs will be reported to the national competent authorities, to the ethics committees and other administrative bodies according to the national regulatory requirements. The required reporting will be carried out by the PV-department at the KKS Heidelberg.

7.8 Management of Post Treatment Symptoms

If clinical symptoms deteriorate, the subject must be seen and assessed by a physician immediately. This does not exclude the subject from the study and follow-up should continue. All subjects entered into the study remain in the study until the study follow-up period has been reached, or subject is withdrawn.

7.9 Data and Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB)

An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) will review aggregate data adjudicate study safety endpoints. The DSMB will adjudicate the occurrence of clinical study safety endpoints in accordance with the definitions specified within this protocol. Planned meetings will occur throughout the study.

The DSMB will review, on a regular basis, all aggregate study data available (i.e. adverse events) and advise Sponsor regarding the continuing safety of the subjects and those yet to be recruited into the study, and as to the continuing validity and scientific merit of the trial. DSMB Charter, generated by the designated DSMB Chairperson and membership will identify the frequency of meetings, identify criteria for early review safety, criteria for stopping the study early and criteria for inactivating sites for excessive protocol deviations. Members of the DSMB will not be affiliated with Sponsor and will declare any conflicts of interest should they arise.

8 Quality Control and Quality Assurance

8.1 Organizational Preparations

A site visit will be performed by Sponsor or their designee prior to the start of the study to review the study protocol in detail and ensure the availability of appropriately trained personnel to conduct the study according to this protocol and under the principles described in the Declaration of Helsinki and the Code of Federal Regulations.

8.2 Training

In order to provide for safe use of devices for thrombectomy the primary concern in operator selection for this study is adequate experience, commitment to safety, and consistency in adherence to the clinical protocol. Therefore, the interventional investigators selected to participate will be neurointerventionalists who, by virtue of their experience and training, are accomplished in cerebral flow restoration techniques using neurothrombectomy devices in intracranial arteries. Each qualified center must conduct at least 50 neurothrombectomy procedures each year. Each interventional investigator will be experienced, and will have participated in at least 50 neurothrombectomy procedures overall. For every thrombectomy device used in the study, at least 15 previous procedures must have been performed by the interventionalist.

8.3 Data Quality Assurance

Training of appropriate clinical site personnel will be the responsibility of the Sponsor or designee. To ensure uniform data collection and protocol compliance, personnel from the Sponsor or designee will review the techniques for the identification of eligible subjects, instructions on in-hospital data collection, methods for soliciting data from alternative sources, and schedules for follow-up with the study center.

8.4 Case Report Forms

Electronic Case Report Forms (eCRFs) in an electronic data collection (EDC) system will be used to collect all subject data during the study. Detailed description of the eCRF components and eCRF completion instructions are included in the Manual of Operations.

The eCRFs must be fully completed for each subject, so that they are available for review in the EDC system by Sponsor appointed monitors within 15 working days of the visit they document.

Trial findings stored on a computer will be stored in accordance with local data protection law and will be handled in strictest confidence. For protection of these data, organizational procedures are implemented to prevent distribution of data to unauthorized persons. The appropriate regulations of local data legislation will be fulfilled in its entirety.

The purpose of monitoring the study is to verify that the rights and well-being of subjects are protected, that the reported study data are accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents and that the conduct of the study is in compliance. Ongoing site monitoring will be conducted by Sponsor or designees to ensure compliance to the protocol and applicable laws and/or regulatory requirements.

The investigator and his/her staff will be expected to cooperate with the Study Monitor, and to be available during at least a portion of the monitoring visits to answer questions and to provide any missing information.

Source data verification (SDV) is an essential element to ensure accuracy and credibility of the data and conclusions derived from clinical investigations. All electronic Case Report Forms will be reviewed for completeness and clarity by the study monitor. Entries on the eCRF are verified using the original documents of SDV such as subject files, informed consent forms signed by subjects, and original recordings from automated instruments, X-ray films, laboratory notes, etc. whereby subject privacy is to be respected. Missing or unclear data shall be supplied or clarified as necessary throughout the study. Sponsor or designee may request further documentation such as physician and/or radiology suite procedure notes when adverse events are observed and reported. Key Study Data will be 100% SDVed, while for all other data a risk adapted approach will be used to guide SDV. Details will be provided in the monitoring plan.

Major amendments require full approval of the EC/IRB and Sponsor prior to implementation, except in emergency situations. All amendments will be distributed to all protocol recipients with instructions.

In situations requiring departure from the protocol, the investigator or other physician in attendance will contact Sponsor. If possible, this contact will be made before implementing any departure from the protocol. In all cases, contact with Sponsor must be made as soon as possible in order to discuss the situation and agree on an appropriate course of action. The protocol deviation eCRF and source document will describe any violation from the protocol and the circumstances.

It is the responsibility of principle investigators to inform their EC/IRB of all protocol amendments or modifications and protocol deviations as required by their EC/IRB procedure and country law.

If it is discovered that an investigator is not complying with the signed agreement, the protocol, or other applicable regulations, or any conditions of approval imposed by the reviewing EC/IRB or regulatory authorities, Sponsor is required to promptly either secure compliance or terminate the investigator's participation in the investigation.

A trained and properly authorized employee of Sponsor or designee, as well as regulatory authorities may request access to all study records, including source documents, for inspection and copying.

9 Ethics and Regulatory Considerations

9.1 Ethics Committee (EC) and Institutional Review Board (IRB)

This study will be carried out according to the study protocol, the Declaration of Helsinki from October 2013, the national regulatory requirements/guidelines of the participating countries concerning Clinical Trials [e.g. the German Medical Device Act (MPG), whereas the § 23b will apply], general national regulatory requirements, e.g. data protection laws and the guidelines of good clinical practice (ICH-GCP).

The protocol and informed consent must be submitted to the appropriate EC/IRB and other requirements per country law, and written approval must be obtained and submitted to Sponsor prior to enrolling any subjects.

The investigator will promptly report to the EC/RB Board all changes in activity and all unanticipated problems involving risks to human subjects or others, and will not make any changes in the research without completing any necessary EC/IRB approval steps, except when necessary to eliminate immediate hazards to human subjects.

Note: The EC/IRB (if required) and Sponsor should be notified of any protocol deviation to protect the life or physical well-being of a subject in an emergency within 24 hours but in no instance later than 5 working days after the emergency occurred.

The investigator must report to the EC/IRB at least yearly on the progress of the investigation, if required. A letter from the EC/IRB should document continuing EC/IRB review. Notification to the EC/IRB by the investigator within 3 months after completion, termination, or discontinuation of the study at the specific site must be documented.

Other investigator responsibilities to the EC/IRB and sponsor include the following:

During the conduct of the study, submit progress reports to the EC/IRB as required.

As required, obtain approval from the EC/IRB for protocol amendments and for revisions to the informed consent or subject recruitment advertisements. A copy of the correspondence should be provided to the study monitor.

Notify the EC/IRB (if required) and Sponsor of any protocol deviation to protect the life or physical well-being of a subject in an emergency within 24 hours but in no instance later than 5 days after the emergency occurred.

Provide EC/IRB with any other information it requests before or during the conduct of the study.

Maintain a file of study-related information that includes all correspondence with the EC/IRB

Notify EC/IRB within 3 months after study completion, termination or discontinuation.

Notify Sponsor, within 72 hours, of withdrawal of approval by the reviewing EC/IRB.
