[DOCLING_PAGE 132]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

7 Safety and Adverse Events

7.1 Adverse Events and Serious Adverse Events: Definitions

An Adverse Event (AE) is any untoward medical occurrence in a study subject, user or other person, and which does not necessarily have a causal relationship with the product or treatment; any unfavorable and unintended sign, symptom, injury, or disease temporally associated with the treatment. A preexisting condition should not be reported as an adverse event unless the condition worsens or episodes increase in frequency during the adverse event reporting period.

A Serious Adverse Event (SAE) includes any of the following events that may or may not be considered related to the treatment according to the GCP and ISO 14155;

Led to a death,

Led to a serious deterioration in health that resulted in:

a life-threatening illness or injury, or

a permanent impairment of a body structure or a body function, or

patient hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization, or medical or surgical intervention to prevent life threatening illness or injury or permanent impairment to a body structure or a body function.

medical or surgical intervention to prevent life-threatening illness or injury or permanent impairment to a body structure or a body function,

Led to fetal distress, fetal death or a congenital abnormality or birth defect.

Otherwise medically relevant

7.2 Documentation of Adverse Events

Each subject in the study will be questioned about non-serious adverse events (AE) until the day of hospital discharge (or 7 days, whichever occurs first) and about serious adverse events (SAE) until the 90 days. Any time during the clinical investigation, the subject may volunteer information that resembles an AE. The investigator will record the nature, severity, treatment and outcome of the AE, and will determine the relationship to device, study-specific treatment or underlying disease.

The severity of an AE refers to the extent of which the patient is impaired in his daily routine. The following categories of adverse event severity are to be used:

Grade 1 Mild AE Signs and symptoms which can be easily tolerated. Symptoms can be ignored or disappear when the subject is distracted.

Grade 2 Moderate AE: Symptoms cause discomfort but are tolerable, they cannot be

36

[DOCLING_PAGE 133]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

ignored and affect normal activity.

Grade 3 Severe AE: Symptoms strongly affect normal activity.

Grade 4

Life-threatening or disabling AE

Grade 5 Death related to AE

All AEs grade 3 to 5 will be documented in the site´s source documents and subject to eCRF documentation (whether or not related to the IMP). AEs grade 1 or 2 do not have to be documented in the eCRF. All required treatments and outcomes of theses AEs must be recorded as well.

All documented AEs should be followed until they are resolved, become stable or chronic, or subject's participation in the study ends. If at the time of the 90-day follow-up visit there is an AE which started during the study, but has not yet resolved, the investigator should make all reasonable efforts to determine its eventual outcome afterwards.

Adverse Events (AE) may or may not be related to the study-specific treatment. An Investigator must make the assessment of relationship to the treatment for each AE. The Investigator should decide whether, in his or her medical judgment, there is a reasonable possibility that the event may have been caused by the study-specific treatment. To evaluate the causality of an AE in regards to the study treatment following consideration should be applied:

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=2 columns=2]
| Related (reasonable possibility) | The temporal relationship between the event and the treatment is compelling and/or follows a known or suspected response pattern to the study-specific treatment, and the event cannot be explained by subject's medical condition, other therapies, or accident. |
| Not related | The event can be readily explained by other factors such as the subject's underlying medical condition, concomitant therapy, or accident and no plausible temporal or biologic relationship exists between the study- specific treatment and the event |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

The outcome of an AE will be classified as follows:

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=5 columns=2]
| Recovered/resolved: | All signs and symptoms of an AE disappeared without any sequels at the time of the last interrogation. |
| Not recovered/not resolved: | Signs and symptoms of an AE are mostly unchanged or worsened at the time of the last interrogation. |
| Recovered/resolved with sequel: | Actual signs and symptoms of an AE disappeared but there are sequels related to the AE. |
| Fatal: | Resulting in death. If there is more than one AE only the AE leading to death (possibly related) will be characterized as 'fatal'. |
| Unknown: | The outcome is unknown or implausible and the information cannot be supplemented or verified. |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

37

[DOCLING_PAGE 134]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021 TENSION

7.3 Anticipated Adverse Events

Anticipated AE are events the nature of which is consistent with the safety information contained in device documentation, i.e. in the Instructions for Use, IFU of the medical device used for the study-specific procedures. 'Anticipated' definitely does not mean that the event may be expected to occur in the course of the underlying disease .

The assessment if an event is anticipated or not will be carried out by the investigator as well as be the second assessor.

The following list of adverse events/complications (derived from IFUs) must be taken into account during assessment of 'anticipatedness'. Only events listed below are anticipated :

Hematoma and haemorrhage at puncture site

Perforation or dissection of the vessel

Vasoconstriction (Vasospasm)

Change in mental status

Persistent neurological deficits

Neurologic deterioration including stroke progression and stroke in new vascular territory

Brain edema

Ischemia

Infection

Allergic reactions

Air embolism

Intracerebral/intracranial haemorrhage

Vascular occlusion

Pseudo aneurysm formation

Aneurysm perforation

Post procedure bleeding

Distal embolization including to a previous uninvolved territory

Adverse reaction/anaphylaxis to antiplatelet/anticoagulation agents

Adverse reaction (anaphylaxis, kidney damage) to contrast media

Device(s) deformation, collapse, fracture or malfunction

Thrombosis (acute and subacute)

Inability to completely remove thrombus

Arteriovenous fistula

Seizure

Pain/headache

7.4 (Unanticipated) Serious Adverse Device Effects

SAEs which are related to the medical device are referred to as Serious Adverse Device Effects ( SADE ).

TENSION

SADE which are unanticipated are referred to as Unanticipated Serious Adverse Device Effects ( USADE ).

7.5 Reporting of Serious Adverse Events by the Investigator

All SAEs occurring between start of study treatment (day 0) and day 90 have to be reported to the PV-department at KKS .

The timeline of reporting depends on two characteristics of SAEs, i.e. on causality and on ' anticipatedness '.

USADEs (Unanticipated Serious Adverse Effects), i.e. those SAEs which are both unanticipated and related to the study-specific treatment will be reported immediately but at the latest within 24 hours of investigator's first awareness about the event.

All other SAEs will be reported at the latest within 7 days of investigator's first awareness about the event.

Reporting will be carried out by faxing the SAE-form to the PV-department at KKS Heidelberg:

Fax-number: 0049 (0)6221- 5633725

The report needs to be as complete as possible and contain information regarding: identification of the clinical trial anonymized subject identification, description of the event (start date, intensity, assessment of the causal relationship and 'anticipatedness', treatment of the SAE and outcome). The reports have to be dated and signed by the investigator.

The PV-department at KKS Heidelberg is responsible for registering all incoming SAE, checking on their completeness, general correctness, and plausibility.

The following events are not considered SAEs and therefore need not to be reported to the PV-department of KKS Heidelberg:

Hospitalization due to diagnostic or elective surgical procedures for pre-existing condition, unless the condition becomes more severe or increases in frequency

Hospitalization for therapy of the study target disease explicitly anticipated and defined in the protocol

7.6 Second Assessment

All SAEs will be subject to a second assessment.

The second assessment includes re-evaluation of causality and 'anticipatedness', as well as appraisal of the influence of a given SAE on the benefit-risk assessment of the trial.

39

[DOCLING_PAGE 136]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

The second assessment will be performed by (a) designated person(s), who will be independent from the reporting investigator.

The second assessor will fill out a 'Second Assessment Sheet' for each SAE and send it back per fax to the PV-department at the KKS Heidelberg within 48 hours:

Fax-number: 0049 (0)6221- 5633725

7.7 Expedited Reporting by the Sponsor

All SAEs will be reported to the national competent authorities, to the ethics committees and other administrative bodies according to the national regulatory requirements. The required reporting will be carried out by the PV-department at the KKS Heidelberg.

7.8 Management of Post Treatment Symptoms

If clinical symptoms deteriorate, the subject must be seen and assessed by a physician immediately. This does not exclude the subject from the study and follow-up should continue. All subjects entered into the study remain in the study until the study follow-up period has been reached, or subject is withdrawn.

7.9 Data and Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB)

An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) will review aggregate data adjudicate study safety endpoints. The DSMB will adjudicate the occurrence of clinical study safety endpoints in accordance with the definitions specified within this protocol. Planned meetings will occur throughout the study.

The DSMB will review, on a regular basis, all aggregate study data available (i.e. adverse events) and advise Sponsor regarding the continuing safety of the subjects and those yet to be recruited into the study, and as to the continuing validity and scientific merit of the trial. DSMB Charter, generated by the designated DSMB Chairperson and membership will identify the frequency of meetings, identify criteria for early review safety, criteria for stopping the study early and criteria for inactivating sites for excessive protocol deviations. Members of the DSMB will not be affiliated with Sponsor and will declare any conflicts of interest should they arise.

8 Quality Control and Quality Assurance

8.1 Organizational Preparations

A site visit will be performed by Sponsor or their designee prior to the start of the study to review the study protocol in detail and ensure the availability of appropriately trained personnel to conduct the study according to this protocol and under the principles described in the Declaration of Helsinki and the applicable EU and national regulations.. For participating countries outside the EU, the national regulations apply.

[DOCLING_PAGE 137]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

8.2 Training

In order to provide for safe use of devices for thrombectomy the primary concern in operator selection for this study is adequate experience, commitment to safety, and consistency in adherence to the clinical protocol. Therefore, the interventional investigators selected to participate will be neurointerventionalists who, by virtue of their experience and training, are accomplished in cerebral flow restoration techniques using neurothrombectomy devices in intracranial arteries. Each qualified center must conduct at least 50 neurothrombectomy procedures each year. Each interventional investigator will be experienced, and will have participated in at least 50 neurothrombectomy procedures overall. For every thrombectomy device used in the study, at least 15 previous procedures must have been performed by the interventionalist.

8.3 Data Quality Assurance

Training of appropriate clinical site personnel will be the responsibility of the Sponsor or designee. To ensure uniform data collection and protocol compliance, personnel from the Sponsor or designee will review the techniques for the identification of eligible subjects, instructions on inhospital data collection, methods for soliciting data from alternative sources, and schedules for follow-up with the study center.

8.4 Case Report Forms

Electronic Case Report Forms (eCRFs) in an electronic data collection (EDC) system will be used to collect all subject data during the study. Detailed description of the eCRF components and eCRF completion instructions are included in the Manual of Operations.

The eCRFs must be fully completed for each subject, so that they are available for review in the EDC system by Sponsor appointed monitors within 15 working days of the visit they document.

8.4.1 Data Reporting

The Principal Investigator, or an individual designated by him/her, is responsible for recording all data from the study on the eCRFs supplied by Sponsor in the EDC system. The Principal Investigator is required to electronically sign the eCRFs on the appropriate page(s) to verify that he/she has reviewed the entered data.

8.5 Data Protection

The data obtained in the course of the trial will be treated pursuant to the EU General Data Protection Regulation (EU GDPR; EU Datenschutz-Grundverordnung (DSGVO)).

During the clinical trial, patients will be identified solely by means of their individual identification code (patient number). Trial findings stored on a computer will be stored in accordance with local data protection law and will be handled in strictest confidence. For protection of these data, organizational procedures are implemented to prevent distribution of data to unauthorized persons. The appropriate regulations of local data legislation will be fulfilled in its entirety.

The patient consents in writing to release the investigator from his/her professional discretion in so far as to allow inspection of original data for monitoring purposes by health authorities and authorized persons (inspectors, monitors, auditors). Authorized persons (clinical monitors, auditors, inspectors) may inspect the patient-related data collected during the trial ensuring the data protection law.

The investigator will maintain a patient identification list (patient numbers with the corresponding patient names) to enable records to be identified.

Patients who did not consent to circulate their pseudonymized data will not be included into the trial.

[DOCLING_PAGE 139]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021 TENSION

This protocol, the eCRFs and other trial-related documents and material must be handled with strict confidentiality and not be disclosed to third parties except with the express prior consent of Sponsor. Staffs of the investigators involved in this study are also bound by this agreement.

8.6 Monitoring and Source Data Verification

A Sponsor appointed Clinical Research Associate (CRA, also be referred to as Study Monitor) will verify completed eCRFs in the EDC system at regular intervals throughout the study, as detailed in the Sponsor's Monitoring plan. To this end, the Principal Investigator must permit inspection of the study files, and subject medical records by Sponsor appointed Study Monitor and authorized government agencies.

The purpose of monitoring the study is to verify that the rights and well-being of subjects are protected, that the reported study data are accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents and that the conduct of the study is in compliance. Ongoing site monitoring will be conducted by Sponsor or designees to ensure compliance to the protocol and applicable laws and/or regulatory requirements.

The investigator and his/her staff will be expected to cooperate with the Study Monitor, and to be available during at least a portion of the monitoring visits to answer questions and to provide any missing information.

Source data verification (SDV) is an essential element to ensure accuracy and credibility of the data and conclusions derived from clinical investigations. All electronic Case Report Forms will be reviewed for completeness and clarity by the study monitor. Entries on the eCRF are verified using the original documents of SDV such as subject files, informed consent forms signed by subjects, and original recordings from automated instruments, X-ray films, laboratory notes, etc. whereby subject privacy is to be respected. Missing or unclear data shall be supplied or clarified as necessary throughout the study. Sponsor or designee may request further documentation such as physician and/or radiology suite procedure notes when adverse events are observed and reported. Key Study Data will be 100% SDVed, while for all other data a risk adapted approach will be used to guide SDV. Details will be provided in the monitoring plan.

Clinical site personnel will perform primary data collection based on source-documented hospital chart reviews.

8.7 Data Management

Clinical site personnel will enter data into eCRFs in the EDC system provided by Sponsor for study data collection. Sponsor or designee will be responsible for confirming the overall integrity of the data.

8.8 Protocol Amendment and Modifications

All items in this protocol are to be followed exactly. If an amendment is required, this must be made in written form and receive approval from all persons and authorities who approved the original protocol. Administrative changes (not affecting subjects' benefits/risks ratio) may be inserted with abbreviated approval by the EC/IRB. Major amendments require full approval of the EC/IRB and Sponsor prior to implementation, except in emergency situations. All amendments will be distributed to all protocol recipients with instructions.

In situations requiring departure from the protocol, the investigator or other physician in attendance will contact Sponsor. If possible, this contact will be made before implementing any departure from the protocol. In all cases, contact with Sponsor must be made as soon as possible in order to discuss the situation and agree on an appropriate course of action. The protocol deviation eCRF and source document will describe any violation from the protocol and the circumstances.

It is the responsibility of principle investigators to inform their EC/IRB of all protocol amendments or modifications and protocol deviations as required by their EC/IRB procedure and country law.

8.9 Securing Compliance

If it is discovered that an investigator is not complying with the signed agreement, the protocol, or other applicable regulations, or any conditions of approval imposed by the reviewing EC/IRB or regulatory authorities, Sponsor is required to promptly either secure compliance or terminate the investigator's participation in the investigation.

8.10 On-Site Audits

A trained and properly authorized employee of Sponsor or designee, as well as regulatory authorities may request access to all study records, including source documents, for inspection and copying.

This study will be carried out according to the study protocol, the Declaration of Helsinki from October 2013, the national regulatory requirements/guidelines of the participating countries concerning Clinical Trials [e.g. the German Medical Device Act (MPG), whereas the § 23b will apply], general national regulatory requirements, e.g. data protection laws and the guidelines of good clinical practice (ICH-GCP).

The investigator will promptly report to the EC/RB Board all changes in activity and all unanticipated problems involving risks to human subjects or others, and will not make any changes in the research without completing any necessary EC/IRB approval steps, except when necessary to eliminate immediate hazards to human subjects.

Note: The EC/IRB (if required) and Sponsor should be notified of any protocol deviation to protect the life or physical well-being of a subject in an emergency within 24 hours but in no instance later than 5 working days after the emergency occurred.

The investigator must report to the EC/IRB at least yearly on the progress of the investigation, if required. A letter from the EC/IRB should document continuing EC/IRB review. Notification to the EC/IRB by the investigator within 3 months after completion, termination, or discontinuation of the study at the specific site must be documented.

Other investigator responsibilities to the EC/IRB and sponsor include the following:

During the conduct of the study, submit progress reports to the EC/IRB as required.

As required, obtain approval from the EC/IRB for protocol amendments and for revisions to the informed consent or subject recruitment advertisements. A copy of the correspondence should be provided to the study monitor.

Notify the EC/IRB (if required) and Sponsor of any protocol deviation to protect the life or physical well-being of a subject in an emergency within 24 hours but in no instance later than 5 days after the emergency occurred.

Provide EC/IRB with any other information it requests before or during the conduct of the study.

Maintain a file of study-related information that includes all correspondence with the EC/IRB

Notify EC/IRB within 3 months after study completion, termination or discontinuation.

Notify Sponsor, within 72 hours, of withdrawal of approval by the reviewing EC/IRB.

9.2 Informed Consent

9.2.1 The normal case and the problem of patients unable to give informed consent

Informed consent has to be obtained prior to enrolment of patients in TENSION. In the normal case written consent will be given by the patient after detailed information about the clinical trial by the investigator. However, given the sample to be studied (i.e. acute stroke patients showing neurological deficits that may comprise aphasia or disturbance of consciousness) we expect that a certain proportion of patients (estimated 40-60%) will be unable to give informed consent. Excluding this group of patients from the clinical trial would entail severe scientific and clinical drawbacks. Results would be biased by systematically excluding more severe strokes and patients with aphasia, i.e. mostly left hemispheric strokes. As a consequence, the generalizability of the study results would be limited. Strictly speaking, in case of a positive result this could not be transferred to the patients excluded from the trial (e.g. aphasic patients). This large group would still be excluded from this effective treatment.

45

[DOCLING_PAGE 142]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021 TENSION

9.2.2 Enrolment of patients unable to give informed consent -general considerations

A possible solution would allow the enrolment of patients unable to give informed consent based on their alleged will following pre-specified criteria. These criteria would have to consider a potential individual benefit as well as any possible risk of participation in the clinical trial. In general the protection of the patient and his autonomy has to be weighed against the potential individual benefit as well as the scientific interest. Following European and national regulations the enrolment of patient (temporarily) unable to give informed consent into clinical trials is possible in an emergency situation if a legal guardian is not available and - after careful consideration of possible risks and potential individual benefit - a participation in the clinical trial would comply with the patient's presumptive will. Moreover, standard treatment following best medical practice must not be withheld to the patient by enrolment in the clinical trial.

For patients eligible for enrolment in TENSION best medical practice in these patients usually comprises i.e. thrombolysis (within 4.5 hours, if indicated) and hospitalization and monitoring in a specialized stroke unit. Standard treatment consists of supervision and modification of physiological parameters (such as blood pressure, body temperature, blood glucose) and early secondary prevention (e.g. Aspirin).

9.2.3 Potential individual benefit and risk of participation in TENSION

Patients enrolled in TENSION will be randomized based on a 1:1 ration to treatment with best medical care alone or best medical care plus thrombectomy. All patients receive standard treatment according to European and national guidelines (see above) independent from the result of randomization. In addition to standard treatment, there is a 50% chance to receive treatment with thrombectomy. This treatment brings a potential benefit for the patient (as the hypothesis to be tested in TENSION which was based on promising results from randomized trials in patients with good prognostic factors treated with thrombectomy will have a good outcome 90 days after stroke). Two recent randomized clinical trials showed that endovascular therapy compared to standard medical therapy alone in an extended time window up to 16 or 24 hours after patient last know well was associated with a higher percentage of patients who were functionally independent and in a favorable mRS shift (Nogueira et al. 2018, Albers et al. 2018). So there is a potential individual benefit foreseeable by participating in TENSION. However, so far no randomized control trial showed a clinical benefit in patients with large infarcts and in an extended time window undergoing thrombectomy compared to best medical care.

The potential risk of participation in the clinical trial can be estimated based on the risk of the thrombectomy procedure. All recent trials (Synthesis, IMS 3, MR Rescue, MR CLEAN, ESCAPE, EXTEND -IA, SWIFT Prime) comparing thrombectomy to standard care all safety endpoints did not differ between treatment groups. Therefore, no major additional risk is associated with the inclusion in the thrombectomy arm in TENSION.

46

[DOCLING_PAGE 143]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

9.2.4 Suggestion for enrolment of patients unable to give informed consent in TENSION

Based on the considerations above following suggestions for enrolment of patients in TENSION are made:

A - Patient is able to give informed consent and able to provide written consent (normal case)

Patient is informed by investigator, patient information is handed over to the patient, and patient provides written consent

B - Patient is able to give informed consent but unable to provide written consent

Patient is informed by investigator, patient information is handed over to the patient, and patient provides witnessed oral consent, which will be documented. Later patient provides written consent as soon as possible

C - Patient is unable to give informed consent, legal guardian is available

Legal guardian acts on behalf of the patient; legal guardian is informed by investigator, patient information is handed over to the legal guardian, legal guardian provides written consent

D - Patient is unable to give informed consent; legal guardian is not available, enrolment of patient by consensus between investigator and independent physician

If the patient is unable to give informed consent and no legal guardian has been appointed, the patient may be enrolled by consensus between the investigator and an independent physician. If possible, patient's next of kin should be contacted to appraise the patient's presumed will; the decision has to be documented using a special form. Immediately parallel an application for appointing a legal guardian will be started later, patient or legal guardian will be informed by the investigator and may or may not provide written consent. This mode of consent will be applied to minimize inclusion bias. Otherwise, especially older and more severely affected patients would be excluded from the trial (Flaherty et al. 2008, Mendyk et al. 2015, Hotter et al. 2017, Thomalla et al. 2018). TENSION aims to include patients with moderately sever to severe stroke. A large majority of these patients will have diminished decision-making capacity due to a reduced level of consciousness, aphasia or other cognitive disorders. Only very few patients will be accompanied by a legal guardian at the time of hospital admission. Since acute ischemic stroke is a medical emergency the treatment decision cannot be postponed to await the appointment of a legal guardian. Excluding these specific subgroups of stroke patients would result in a study population that is not representative of acute stroke patients in general. It would also lead to a systematic exclusion of patients with certain types of neurological deficit (i.e. aphasia). Therefore, exclusion of this patient subgroup would lead to a loss of generalizability and slower recruitment rates.

Notification procedures and checklists for the investigator will be in place in order to assure the obtainment of the subsequent patient´s or legal guardian`s informed consent in a fixed timeline.

These options for obtaining the informed consent are NOT equal or interchangeable. The highest priority will always be to obtain informed consent by the subject himself. Only if this option is not possible, other ways may be approached.

47

[DOCLING_PAGE 144]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

Please see Appendix 2 'Overview of Ways of Obtaining Informed Consent' for further visual clarification.

9.3 Confidentiality

All information and data sent to Sponsor concerning subjects or their participation in this study will be considered confidential. All information and data captured in EDC system or sent to Sponsor will be de-identified information. All data used in the analysis and reporting of this evaluation will be used in a manner without identifiable reference to the subject. The Principal Investigator consents to visits by the staff of the Sponsor and its authorized representatives as well as regulatory authorities, which governs the conduct of clinical investigations.

9.4 Other Ethical Considerations

Study participants will not receive any reimbursement; however, reasonable travel expenses for any visits additional to normal care will be reimbursed on production of receipts, or a distance allowance of €0.30 per kilometer.

9.5 Insurance

Treatment within the trial will be made according to current guidelines and using available and approved medical devices. Any potential injury or damage to patients related to treatment within the trial is thus covered by the insurances of participating trial sites. Details will be laid down in the clinical trial agreements. Additional accident insurance will be provided for study participants who travel to the site for the 90 day follow-up examination.

The additional accident insurance was taken out at HDI Global SE (Accident Insurance Number 570 10310-03705). The terms and conditions should be handed out to the study participants / their legal representative.

9.6 Funding

TENSION is an investigator initiated trial. Sponsor is the University of Heidelberg. TENSION will be funded by the European Commission (EC) within Horizon 2020. There is no funding or other involvement by pharmaceutical industry or medical device companies.

12 End of Study

12.1 Planned End

The study will close when all subjects have completed the 12 month follow up visit or have discontinued prematurely.

12.2 Premature End

The study may be discontinued or put on hold because:

New findings invalidate the earlier positive benefit-risk assessment.

The time schedule (recruitment phase) cannot be met (e.g. more than doubling of recruitment time).

Sponsor decides to discontinue the study at any time.

Interim analysis indicates early success or futility

12.3 Termination of Site by Sponsor

Sponsor reserves the right to terminate an investigational site; this may include for any of the following reasons (but not be limited to):

The center cannot comply with the requirements of the protocol

Repeated failure to complete case report forms

Failure to report SAE, UADE, or subject death within 3 working days of knowledge

Repeated protocol violations

Investigator leaves the institution and no adequate replacement is available.

15 Appendix 1: Study Definitions

Dissection: A flow-limiting tear or flap in the arterial wall

Fever: Documented temperature > 38˚C

Groin hematoma: An accumulation of blood at the puncture site requiring evacuation, transfusion, or extended hospital stay

Headache: Cerebral pain requiring medication

Hemorrhage: Acute loss of blood requiring transfusion

Intracerebral Hemorrhage (ICH): Two types -

Hemorrhagic Infarct: Any area of petechial or small, confluent hemorrhages within larger regions of hypodense, ischemic injury without mass effect.

Parenchymal Hemorrhage (PH): More homogenous areas of hemorrhage, with or without intraventricular extension, usually with mass effect. PH1 is defined as hematoma in <30 % of infarct area. PH2 is defined as hematoma in >30% of infarct area

Neurological Deterioration : An increase in NIHSS score of ≥ 4 points from baseline or most recent assessment.

Stroke: Signs of focal cerebral or retinal ischemia persisting for 24 hours or more. A stroke is considered disabling if the modified Rankin score is 3 or greater

Subarachnoid Hemorrhage: Bleeding from a ruptured intracranial blood vessel, confined to the subarachnoid space

Symptomatic Intracranial Hemorrhage (SITS-MOST): SICH at 30 (-/+ 6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in SITS-MOST ('local or remote parenchymal hematoma type 2 on the imaging scan obtained 22 to 36 hours after treatment, plus neurologic deterioration, as indicated by a score on the NIHSS ≥ 4 points than the baseline value or the lowest value between baseline and 24 hours, or haemorrhage leading to death') (Wahlgren et al., 2007)

Symptomatic Intracranial Hemorrhage (ECASS II): SICH at 30 (-/+ 6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in ECASS II ('any hemorrhage with neurologic deterioration, as indicated by an NIHSS score ≥ 4 points than the value at baseline or the lowest value in the first 7 days, or any hemorrhage leading to death') (Hacke et al., 1998)

Symptomatic Intracranial Hemorrhage (NINDS): SICH at 30 (-/+ 6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in NINDS ('any new hemorrhage associated with any neurological deterioration') (NINDS study group, 1995)

Thrombolysis in Cerebral Infarction (TICI) Flow

TICI 0: No perfusion - No antegrade flow beyond the point of occlusion.

TICI 1: Penetration with minimal perfusion - The contrast material passes beyond the area of obstruction but fails to opacify the entire cerebral bed distal to the obstruction for the duration of the angiographic run.

56

[DOCLING_PAGE 153]

Protocol Version V4.2.2 from 26.01.2021

TENSION

TICI 2: Partial perfusion - The contrast material passes beyond the obstruction and opacifies the arterial bed distal to the obstruction. However, the rate of entry of contrast into the vessel distal to the obstruction and/or its rate of clearance from the distal bed are perceptibly slower than its entry into and/or clearance from comparable areas not perfused by the previously occluded vessel (e.g., the opposite cerebral artery or the arterial bed proximal to the obstruction).

TICI 2a: Only partial filling - <50% of the entire vascular territory is opacified .

TICI 2b: Majority Filling - ≥ 50% of the expected vascular territory is opacified,

TICI 3: Complete perfusion - Antegrade flow into the bed distal to the obstruction occurs as promptly as into the obstruction and clearance of contrast material from the involved bed is as rapid as from an uninvolved other bed of the same vessel or the opposite cerebral artery.

1 PREFACE

This Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) describes the planned analysis and reporting for protocol TENSION entitled 'Efficacy and safety of ThrombEctomy iN Stroke with extended leSION and extended time window: a randomized controlled trial (TENSION)'.

This phase III study is being completed to assess efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with uncertain benefit of endovascular stroke treatment, i.e. extended ischemic lesion size with an ASPECT score of 3-5 up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well as compared to best medical care alone.

The structure and content of this SAP provides sufficient detail to meet the requirements identified by the FDA and International Conference on Harmonization of Technical Requirements for Registration of Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH): Guidance on Statistical Principles in Clinical Trials. All work planned and reported for this SAP will follow internationally accepted guidelines, published by the American Statistical Association and the Royal Statistical Society, for statistical practice.

The following documents were reviewed in preparation of this SAP:

Cinical Research Protocol Version 4.2, 06 August 2019

Case report forms (CRFs).

ICH Guidance on Statistical Principles for Clinical Trials.

The reader of this SAP is encouraged to also read the clinical protocols for details on the

conduct of this study, and the operational aspects of clinical assessments and timing for

completing a patient in this study.

Final version 1.0

[DOCLING_PAGE 162]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

2 PURPOSE OF SAP

The purpose of this SAP is to outline the planned analyses to be completed to support the completion of the Clinical Study Report (CSR). The planned analyses identified in this SAP will be included in regulatory submissions and/or future manuscripts. Also, exploratory analyses not necessarily identified in this SAP may be performed to support the clinical development program. Any post-hoc, or unplanned, analyses not identified in this SAP performed will be clearly identified in the respective CSR.

 Co-Principal investigator

 Biostatistician

After validation, the SAP may be amended and all changes should be clearly tracked. Amendments can occur only before the database is locked. In particular, it may be amended following the final blind review of the data.

4 STUDY OBJECTIVES AND ENDPOINTS

4.1 Study Objectives

The primary objective is to test efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with uncertain benefit of endovascular stroke treatment, i.e. extended ischemic lesion size with an ASPECT score of 3-5 up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well as compared to best medical care alone.

4.2 Study Endpoints (Target Variables)

4.2.1 Primary efficacy endpoints

The primary efficacy endpoint is:

Ordinal mRS outcome at 90 (±14) days post-stroke.

4.2.2 Secondary efficacy endpoints

Secondary clinical efficacy endpoints are:

 Independent neurological outcome (mRS≤2) at 90 (±14) days

 Moderate neurological outcome (mRS≤3) with 90 (±14) days

 Rates of decompressive craniotomy

 Infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) post randomization.

 Difference of infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours from infarct volume as predicted by pretreatment imaging

 Functional neurological outcome at 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (simplified modified Rankin Scale questionnaire, smRSq)

 Patient-reported functional health status and quality of life 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PROMIS-10, EQ-5D-3L)

 Post-stroke depression 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PHQ-4)

Final version 1.0

8

[DOCLING_PAGE 164]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

Secondary imaging efficacy endpoints are:

 Infarct volume at follow-up after 24 (±6) hours will be measured by MRI or CT and compared between the endovascular thrombectomy arm and the best medical care arm, the variable being transformed if necessary.

 Difference from predicted infarct volume will be calculated based on CT perfusion or MR perfusion imaging acquired pre randomization (Forkert, ND et al. 2013).

Secondary angiogenic efficacy endpoints are:

 Rate of embolization, either distal to the target occlusion or to previously unaffected territories

 Rate of successful restoration of flow of the occluded target arterial vessel, defined as TICI 2b or better (thrombectomy arm only)

4.2.3 Safety endpoints

Safety endpoints are:

 Death or dependency (mRS 4-6)

 SICH at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in SITS-MOST

 parenchymal hemorrhage type 2 (PH-2)

 Frequencies of AEs in both treatment arms within the first 7 days after treatment

 Serious AEs (SAEs), overall (all-cause mortality) and stroke-related death (i.e., related to intracranial bleeding, midline shift, brain herniation, progression of stroke symptoms, or systemic complications from stroke such as pneumonia).

 Space-occupying infarction (malignant brain edema)

 New ischemic stroke

Final version 1.0

9

[DOCLING_PAGE 165]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

5 STUDY METHODS

5.1 Overall Study Design and Plan

TENSION is an investigator-initiated, prospective, open label, blinded endpoint (PROBE), multinational, European, two-arm, randomized, controlled, post-market clinical trial. Patients will be randomized 1:1 to either best medical care + endovascular thrombectomy or best medical care alone. It is planned to recruit 665 patients in approximately 40 sites in eight to ten European countries, to be followed-up for 12 months.

5.2 Selection of Study Population

Patients eligible for initial study screening are those who present with symptoms attributable to acute thrombotic occlusion in the M1 segment of the MCA and/or distal ICA treatable within 12 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well. All such patients who meet all general eligibility criteria will be randomized

Clinical Inclusion criteria

 Randomization within 11 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well.

 Endovascular treatment is expected to be finished within 12 hours after known symptom onset or last seen well by judgment of the interventional neuroradiologist in charge

 Patient must demonstrate clinical signs and symptoms attributable to target area of occlusion consistent with the diagnosis of ischemic stroke, including impairment of the following: language, motor function, sensation, cognition, gaze, and/or vision for at least 30 minutes without relevant improvement.

 Female and male patient above 18 up to 80 years of age

 Moderate to severe stroke (NIHSS Score of <26)

Final version 1.0

10

[DOCLING_PAGE 166]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

 Signed informed consent (IC) form by patient or legal guardian, or inclusion of patient's presumptive will by investigator under emergency situation, but after consultation of an independent physician who is familiar with these types of illness if the other options are not possible according to local approval.

 Prior to new focal neurological deficit, mRS score was ≤2.

Imaging Inclusion Criteria

 A new focal occlusion confirmed by imaging (MRA/CTA) to be accessible to the thrombectomy device, and located in the M1 of the middle cerebral artery (MCA) and/or the intracranial segment of the distal internal carotid artery (ICA).

 CT (non-contrast CT) or DWI with an ASPECT score of 3-5

Clinical exclusion criteria

 Patient is an active participant in another drug or device treatment trial for any disease state or patient is expected to start participation in another drug or device treatment trial while enrolled in this protocol, unless approved by Sponsor.

 Patient has pre-existing neurological or psychiatric disease that could impede the study results or would confound the neurological or functional evaluations.

 Patient has history of contraindication for contrast medium.

 Patient is known to have infective endocarditis

 Patient's anticipated life expectancy is less than 12 months

Imaging exclusion criteria

 Extracranial CT or MR angiography showing high grade stenosis expected to require acute stent placement (e.g. ICA stenting) during the procedure

 CT or MRI with evidence of mass effect or intracranial tumor

Final version 1.0

11

[DOCLING_PAGE 167]

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

 If CT perfusion is done per institutional standard: CBV drop on CTP with an ASPECT score of 0-2

5.3 Method of Treatment Assignment and Randomization

Randomization will be done via the eCRF. Subjects will be randomized to one of the following treatment arms: 'best medical care' or 'best medical care plus thrombectomy'

A randomization schedule (1:1 ratio, best medical care alone: best medical care plus thrombectomy) stratified by stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 6-11 hours) will be generated as described in Section 8.

To randomize the subject, an authorized member of the study site staff will access the webbased system with their individual account information, and will, at a minimum, input the subject identification number, NIHSS, and time from symptom onset. The site will then be provided with a treatment assignment for the subject. Only one subject may be randomized at a time. Randomization assignments will be documented.

The randomization list will be prepared by the Independent Statistical Centre at the Hospices Civils de Lyon (France). It will be sent only to the Data Coordinating Centre in charge of central randomization and data management.

6 SEQUENCE OF PLANNED ANALYSES

6.1 Interim Analyses

The sponsor has the right to halt or terminate the study in any case of concern for the safety of the patients resulting from new information.

Two sequential interim analyses are planned after data on one third or two thirds of the total number of patients with a 90-day visit have been collected to monitor early efficacy of

Final version 1.0

12

[DOCLING_PAGE 168]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

treatment effect or futility (rejection of the null hypothesis at the final analysis highly unlikely).

The interim analysis pattern to be used will employ a power cumulative error spending function (Jennison, C. and Turnbull, B. W. (2000) Group Sequential Methods with Applications to Clinical Trials, New York: Chapman & Hall), with a parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility). The corresponding stopping boundaries (Z-values) at the 2 interim analyses and the final analysis are equal to 3.11, 2.46 and 2.01 for the alpha spending function (efficacy), and -2.38, -2.11 and 2.01 for the betapending function (futility). The corresponding cumulative stopping probabilities under the null hypothesis (CRef=0) and alternative hypothesis (CRef=1) are given in the table below:

| CRef | Expected Stopping Stage | Source | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities |

| 0.0000 | 2.526 | Accept Null | 0.00876 | 0.45706 | 0.97500 |

[/DOCLING_TABLE]

The test statistic generated from the ordinal logistic model is the t-statistic for the treatment parameter, calculated by dividing the parameter estimate by its standard error (Dements, Land 1994). If the t-statistic based on the current number of patients is crosses the prespecified limits, the DSMB may recommend suspending the trial for early success or futility.

Final version 1.0

13

[DOCLING_PAGE 169]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

Summary safety reports will be generated and provided to the DSMB for review at prespecified time intervals, at least three times over the course of the study, where the first and second time may not be after the first and second interim analysis, respectively. Details of the DSMB reports and review processes will be defined in the DSMB charter.

These stopping rules should be considered as guidelines. The decision to stop or continue the trial will be based on the overall assessment of risk and benefit.

6.2 Final Analyses and Reporting

All final, planned, analyses identified in the protocol and in this SAP will be performed only after the formal database lock. A blinded data review meeting will be held prior to database lock and completion of the final analyses. In addition, no database may be locked, random code unblinded, or analyses completed until this SAP has been approved.

Key statistics and study results will be made available to the Steering Committee following database lock and prior to completion of the final CSR.

Any, post-hoc, exploratory analyses completed to support planned study analyses, which were not identified in this SAP, will be documented and reported in appendices to the CSR. Any results from these unplanned analyses will also be clearly identified in the text of the CSR.

7 SAMPLE SIZE DETERMINATION

Simulations were carried out using an assumption for the possible true distribution of mRS from the literature (Goyal et al 2015, Supplement: distribution of mRS in patients with an ASPECT score of 7 or less (5)) and a proportional odds alternative with an odds ratio of 1.5 to be assessed using the primary endpoint mRS shift analysis. Outcome profiles were assumed for the treatment arm, as in Table 2 (for each row, table entries are the assumed proportion of subjects in the patient population who would have the indicated mRS outcome if treated with the control or thrombectomy, respectively).

Final version 1.0

14

[DOCLING_PAGE 170]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=3 columns=7]
|  | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5&6 |
| Control | 0.02 | 0.06 | 0.12 | 0.16 | 0.24 | 0.40 |
| Thrombectomy | 0.03 | 0.09 | 0.16 | 0.18 | 0.23 | 0.31 |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

mRS Outcome

Under the assumption of these distributions, a total of 620 patients is required to achieve a power of 80% for a one-sided test at the 0.025 level.

The trial will undergo two interim analyses with possible premature stopping for futility or early success. As a consequence, the total sample size needs to be increased. With a power cumulative error spending function of parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for the alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility), up to a maximum of 633 patients will be required if the trial does not stop early.

Assuming a 5% drop-out rate of patients to assess the primary endpoint obtained 3 months after inclusion an effective sample size of 665 should be planned to be recruited.

8 ANALYSIS POPULATIONS

The following analysis populations are planned for the studies:

Full Analysis Set (EFFICACY): [or Intent-to-Treat] The Efficacy Population includes all patients who were randomized. Patients will be analyzed in the treatment group to which they were allocated by randomization.

Per-Protocol Efficacy (PP-EFFICACY): The PP-Efficacy includes all patients randomized to a study treatment who received the study treatment according to the treatment protocol and who are judged to comply with all clinical and imaging inclusion and exclusion criteria according to the results of central image reading. Patients who were randomized by the local investigators but are judged to violate imaging inclusion or exclusion criteria by the Central Image Reading Board (CIRB) will be excluded from the randomized patients to create the PPEfficacy population.

Final version 1.0

15

[DOCLING_PAGE 171]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

A list of patients to be excluded from the randomized patients to create the PP-Efficacy analysis will be established and validated by the Steering Committee prior to unblinded analysis.

Safety Population (SAFETY): The Safety Population includes all patients who received any amount of a planned study intervention, e.g., in whom thrombectomy was initiated and groin puncture was attempted. In case of violation of the randomization scheme, patients will be classified according to the treatment they actually received.

Patients will be assigned to the different populations prior to unblinded final analysis.

9 GENERAL ISSUES FOR STATISTICAL ANALYSIS

9.1 Analysis Software

All statistical analyses will be performed using SAS ® Software version 9.4 or later in a Windows environment.

9.2 Methods for Withdrawals, Missing Data, and Outliers

All efforts will be made to collect outcome data also in patients withdrawn from the trial for whichever reasons and to minimize the amount of missing data.

9.2.1 Withdrawals

All patients with consent withdrawal will be excluded from the efficacy analysis.

Final version 1.0

16

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

9.2.2 Missing data

Sensitivity analyses based on different hypotheses about the missingness pattern of the primary outcome will be performed to test for the robustness of the primary analysis (see section 'Primary Efficacy Variable Analysis').

9.3 Data Transformations

Analysis of continuous variables may require transformation to normalize the distributions, but these variables will be primarily analyzed using non parametric statistical tests. Decisions to transform the distribution of some variables for analysis may be taken after the blind review of the data, and before unblinding the treatment code.

9.4 Multicenter Studies

No adjustment on centre will be performed for the primary analysis, on the basis of the following rationale:

The TENSION trial is aimed to recruit 660 patients from about 40 centres in 8 to 10 european countries, hence an average of 16 patients per centre. However, it is likely that many centres will recruit far fewer patients, while a small number of big centres will recruit much more. Such a situation is an argument for not adjusting the primary analysis on centres. As ICH9 guideline quotes:

'In some trials, for example some large mortality trials with very few subjects per centre, there may be no reason to expect the centres to have any influence on the primary or secondary variables because they are unlikely to represent influences of clinical importance.'

In addition, the protocol did not plan to randomize patients with stratification on centres. However, the centre effect may be accounted for in secondary analyses, together with other important prognostic covariates.

Final version 1.0

17

[DOCLING_PAGE 173]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

9.5 Multiple Comparisons and Multiplicity

Multiple testing because of repeated sequential analyses on the main efficacy and safety criteria is accounted for to adjust the type-one error rate (see section 5.1).

No adjustment for multiple testing will be made to control the type-one error rate on the other statistical tests. However, the interpretation of any significant test should account for the inflated risk of false positive results due to multiple testing.

9.6 Planned Subgroups, Interactions, and Covariates

Subgroup analyses will be performed on the two stratification variables, i.e. stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 611 hours), and a few other important patient characteristics (see section 13.3 Exploratory analyses for details) .

These analyses are exploratory and will not lead to formal inferential conclusions about the treatment effect in any particular subgroup.

9.7 Derived and Computed Variables

Some derived and computed variables have been initially identified, and described as appropriate later in the document. It is expected that additional variables will be required. The SAP will not be amended for additional variables that are not related to the primary target or key secondary target variables. Any additional derived or computed variables will be identified and documented in the SAS programs that create analysis files.

9.8 Presentation of results

Description of continuous variables includes the mean, standard deviation, median, 1st and 3rd quartile, and range.

Final version 1.0

[DOCLING_PAGE 174]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

Description of categorical variables includes the frequency and percentage.

Description of ordered variables includes the frequency, percentage and cumulative percentage.

9.9 Statistical tests

Unless otherwise specified, between-group differences will be tested using the Wilcoxon rank sum test for continuous outcomes, using the Fisher exact test for binary outcomes, and using the Cochran-Armitage trend test for ordinal outcomes.

13 EFFICACY ANALYSES

Statistical analyses of the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints will primarily be based on the Efficacy (Intent-to-treat) population. Secondarily, the same analyses will be repeated on the Per-protocol population.

All statistical tests will be two-sided tests.

The variables in the source database to be used in the analyses are identified in the text as 'DATASET.VARIABLENAME'

13.1 Primary effectiveness variable Analysis

13.1.1 Formal analysis

The primary effectiveness endpoint for a subject is the blinded evaluation of the ordinal mRS outcome at 90 (+/-14) days post-stroke ( MRS.QSORRES, SVSEQ=7).

The primary effectiveness endpoint analysis (referred to as a 'mRS shift' analysis) is carried out as a parameter test in a proportional-odds logistic regression model with the mRS ordinal scale as response variable and treatment group, stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) ( NIHSS.NIHSS_TOTAL, SVSEQ=1) , time from symptom onset until randomization (0-6 hours and 6-11hours)( IC.STRAT2 ) as explanatory variables. For this mRS shift analysis, ordinal mRS outcomes in categories 5 and 6 are pooled into one category. The null and alternative hypotheses for this primary endpoint are H0: β=0 and HA: β≠0, where β is the

Final version 1.0

22

[DOCLING_PAGE 178]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

parameter for the treatment effect in the logistic model. This model is based on the assumption of proportional odds, with constant underlying treatment effect (odds ratio) at any level of the MRS scale (Agresti, A. (2002), Categorical Data Analysis, Second Edition, New York: John Wiley & Sons). The treatment effect will be tested using the likelihood ratio test of the treatment parameter. The Wald confidence interval of the treatment effect will be provided, with coverage probability in accordance with the nominal alpha level of the final analysis .

In a secondary analysis, the primary analysis will be repeated for the per protocol population.

All MRS data evaluated at the 90-day visit ( SVSEQ=7 ) will be used in the analysis, whatever the actual date of assessment. Missing MRS scores in patients who died before day 90 will be assigned a score of 6.

Missing primary outcome values will be replaced using multiple imputation technics. The model used to impute the missing values will be the logistic regression model including the following covariates: allocated treatment, baseline age (continuous) ( DM.AGE ), baseline NIHSS value ( NIHSS.NIHSS_TOTAL, SVSEQ=1 ), and NIHSS value measured at hospital discharge ( NIHSS.NIHSS_TOTAL, SVSEQ=5 ). A total of 5 multiply imputed datasets will be generated to get the final treatment effect estimate and associated confidence interval.

13.1.2 Sensitivity and complementary analyses

A comparison of the baseline characteristics will be performed to assess any imbalance between patients with or without missing primary endpoint.

The reason for missing primary endpoint will be described by treatment group.

Final version 1.0

23

[DOCLING_PAGE 179]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

In order to assess the influence of the missing primary endpoints on the treatment effect estimate, sensitivity analyses will be performed using the same statistical methods as for the formal analysis, considering two opposite situations:

1/ Worst-case scenario:

All patients with a missing primary endpoint will be considered in the highest category (MRS=5-6) in the active treatment group, and in the lowest category (MRS=0) in the control group (the most unfavorable situation).

2/ Best-case scenario:

All patients with a missing primary endpoint will be considered in the lowest category (MRS=0) in the active treatment group, and in the highest category (MRS=5-6) in the control group (the most favorable situation).

In a complementary analysis, an ordinal logistic regression model will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, retaining the randomization stratification factors, the centre effect entered as a random effect, as well as all clinical and imaging candidate baseline variables associated with the outcome with p-value <0.10.

13.2 Secondary Efficacy Variable Analysis

Independent neurological outcome (mRS≤2) at 90 (±14) days

MRS.QSORRES, SVSEQ=7

An unconditional logistic regression model will be fitted, with the same explanatory variables as for the primary endpoint to estimate the treatment effect parameter (odds-ratio) and its 95% confidence interval.

Moderate neurological outcome (mRS≤2) at 90 (±14) days

MRS.QSORRES, SVSEQ=7

Rates of decompressive craniotomy

An unconditional logistic regression model will be fitted, with the same explanatory variables as for the primary endpoint to estimate the treatment effect parameter (odds-ratio) and its 95% confidence interval.

 Infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) post randomization

Variable to be defined

Core infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours post-treatment based on FLAIR or T2-weighted MRI (preferred) or unenhanced CT if MRI is not feasible, as determined by a blinded core lab. Treatment effect with respect to this endpoint will be estimated using a log-linear model stratified according to the volumetric method and with the same explanatory variables as for the primary endpoint. Patients with decompressive craniotomy within 30 hours will have their highest pre-surgery value substituted for the 30 hour value

 Difference of infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours from infarct volume as predicted by pretreatment imaging

Variables to be defined

The difference between the treatment groups and its 95% confidence interval will be estimated, and tested to zero using a Mann-Whitney U test.

 Patient-reported functional health status and quality of life 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PROMIS-10, EQ-5D-3L)

Each item will be described and compared between the 2 treatment groups using the Cochran-Armitage trend test. The health status assessed with a visual analogic scale ( EQ.EQ6 ) will be compared between the 2 treatment groups using the Mann-Whitney U test.

 Post-stroke depression 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PHQ-4)

Each item will be described and compared between the 2 treatment groups using the Cochran-Armitage trend test.

 Functional neurological outcome at 12 months (±14 days) after stroke

MRS.QSORRES, SVSEQ=8

The same model as used for the analysis of the primary efficacy outcome will be fitted.

 Use of health care system resources

Comparison of the use of health care system resources in the two groups will be performed, using the Student's t-test for continuous items, the Fisher exact test for binary items and the Cochran-Armitage trend test for ordinal items.

13.3 Exploratory analyses

Subgroup analyses will be performed on the two stratification variables, i.e. stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (0-6 hours and 6-11hours).

For each of these variables, a proportional-odds logistic regression model will be fitted, relating the log-odds of the primary outcome with the covariate of interest, its interaction with the treatment group, and the treatment group. The interaction term will be tested with the Wald Chi-squared test. The treatment effect (odds-ratio) and its 95% confidence interval will be estimated for each category of the categorical variable.

These analyses are exploratory in nature and no definitive conclusion on the treatment effect in one or the other subgroups will be drawn.

Final version 1.0

26

[DOCLING_PAGE 182]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

Subgroup analyses will also be performed on the following subgroups:

 Sex (female, male)

 Age (18-49, 50-59, 60-69, 70-79, ≥80); (18-79, ≥80)

 ASPECTS (3, 4, 5); (3-4, 5); (3, 4-5)

 Pretreatment with intravenous alteplase (yes, no)

 Vessel occlusion (ICA, M1)

 Type of anesthesia (general anesthesia, conscious sedation)

14 SAFETY AND TOLERABILITY ANALYSES

14.1 Safety Analysis

Safety analyses will be performed on the Safety population only.

14.1.1 Safety endpoints analysis

The following safety endpoints will be studied:

 Death or dependency 90 (±10) days after stroke (MRS 4-6)

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

Patients with missing outcome will not be included in the regression analysis.

 Mortality rate at discharge and 90 days post-treatment

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect at the two time points, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

Final version 1.0

27

[DOCLING_PAGE 183]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan

ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

In addition, a proportional hazard regression model will be fitted to estimate the hazard ratio associated with treatment effect over the total follow-up time, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

Survival probabilities will be plotted using Kaplan-Meier survival estimates in each treatment group.

 Stroke-related death

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

 Space-occupying infarction (malignant brain edema)

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

 New ischemic stroke

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

 Symptomatic intracranial haemorrhage (SICH) as defined in SITS-MOST (Variable t o be defined )

SICH will be assessed by Safety Adjudication Committee.

The occurrence of SICH will be compared between the test and the control arm. An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors.

14.2 Tolerability Analysis (adverse events)

Tolerability analyses will be performed on the Safety population only.

14.2.1 Adverse Events

Adverse events will be coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) central coding dictionary.

14.2.1.1 All Adverse Events

 Any adverse event during the first 7 days following randomization

Proportions will be compared between treatment groups using the Fisher exact test.

Summaries of incidence rates (frequencies and percentages), intensity, and relationship to study drug of individual AEs by System Organ Class and Preferred Term (MedDRA) will be prepared. Each patient will be counted only once within each category (System Organ Class or Preferred Term). If a patient experiences more than one AE within a category, only the AE with the strongest relationship or the greatest intensity, as appropriate, will be included in the summaries of relationship and intensity.

Summaries will be presented by treatment group for all patients in the Safety Population. Fisher's exact test will be used to compare proportions for System Organ Classes across treatment groups.

Counts of AEs by maximum intensity will be presented for patients in the Safety Population by treatment group. Only AEs beginning at or after the beginning of study drug administration will be included. Similarly, counts of AEs by strongest relationship to study drug will be presented.

Final version 1.0

29

[DOCLING_PAGE 185]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

A data listing of AEs leading to withdrawal will also be provided, displaying details of the event(s) captured on the CRF.

14.2.1.2 Serious Adverse Events

Serious adverse event reconciliation will be performed by Data Management, Clinical Research, and Pharmacovigilance via data listings.

SAEs occurring up to the 90-day visit will be described

A summary of incidence rates (frequencies and percentages) of SAEs by treatment group, System Organ Class, and Preferred Term will be prepared for the Safety Population. No statistical tests will be performed.

A data listing of SAEs will also be provided, displaying details of the event(s) captured on the CRF.

1 PREFACE

This Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) describes the planned analysis and reporting for protocol TENSION entitled 'Efficacy and safety of ThrombEctomy iN Stroke with extended leSION and extended time window: a randomized controlled trial (TENSION)'.

This phase III study is being completed to assess efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with uncertain benefit of endovascular stroke treatment, i.e. extended ischemic lesion size with an ASPECT score of 3-5 up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well as compared to best medical care alone.

The structure and content of this SAP provides sufficient detail to meet the requirements identified by the FDA and International Conference on Harmonization of Technical Requirements for Registration of Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH): Guidance on Statistical Principles in Clinical Trials. All work planned and reported for this SAP will follow internationally accepted guidelines, published by the American Statistical Association and the Royal Statistical Society, for statistical practice.

The following documents were reviewed in preparation of this SAP:

Cinical Research Protocol Version 4.2, 06 August 2019

Case report forms (CRFs).

ICH Guidance on Statistical Principles for Clinical Trials.

The reader of this SAP is encouraged to also read the clinical protocols for details on the

conduct of this study, and the operational aspects of clinical assessments and timing for

completing a patient in this study.

Final version 3.0

6

[DOCLING_PAGE 192]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

2 PURPOSE OF SAP

The purpose of this SAP is to outline the planned analyses to be completed to support the completion of the Clinical Study Report (CSR). The planned analyses identified in this SAP will be included in regulatory submissions and/or future manuscripts. Also, exploratory analyses not necessarily identified in this SAP may be performed to support the clinical development program. Any post-hoc, or unplanned, analyses not identified in this SAP performed will be clearly identified in the respective CSR.

3 VALIDATION OF SAP AND AMENDMENTS

The SAP should be validated and signed by:

 Coordinating investigator (Principal investigator)

 Co-Principal investigator

 Biostatistician

After validation, the SAP may be amended and all changes should be clearly tracked. Amendments can occur only before the database is locked. In particular, it may be amended following the final blind review of the data.
