4 STUDY OBJECTIVES AND ENDPOINTS

4.1 Study Objectives

The primary objective is to test efficacy and safety of thrombectomy in acute stroke patients with uncertain benefit of endovascular stroke treatment, i.e. extended ischemic lesion size with an ASPECT score of 3-5 up to 12 hours from symptom onset or last seen well as compared to best medical care alone.

4.2 Study Endpoints (Target Variables)

4.2.1 Primary efficacy endpoints

The primary efficacy endpoint is:

Ordinal mRS outcome at 90 (±14) days post-stroke.

4.2.2 Secondary efficacy endpoints

Secondary clinical efficacy endpoints are:

 Independent neurological outcome (mRS≤2) at 90 (±14) days

 Moderate neurological outcome (mRS≤3) with 90 (±14) days

 Rates of decompressive craniotomy

 Infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) post randomization.

 Difference of infarct volume at 24 (±6) hours from infarct volume as predicted by pretreatment imaging

 Functional neurological outcome at 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (simplified modified Rankin Scale questionnaire, smRSq)

Final version 3.0

9

[DOCLING_PAGE 195]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

 Patient-reported functional health status and quality of life 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PROMIS-10, EQ-5D-3L)

 Post-stroke depression 90 (±14) days and 12 months (±14 days) after stroke (PHQ-4)

 Overall survival

Secondary imaging efficacy endpoints are:

 Infarct volume at follow-up after 24 (±6) hours will be measured by MRI or CT and compared between the endovascular thrombectomy arm and the best medical care arm, the variable being transformed if necessary.

 Difference from predicted infarct volume will be calculated based on CT perfusion or MR perfusion imaging acquired pre randomization (Forkert, ND et al. 2013).

Secondary angiogenic efficacy endpoints are:

 Rate of embolization, either distal to the target occlusion or to previously unaffected territories

 Rate of successful restoration of flow of the occluded target arterial vessel, defined as TICI 2b or better (thrombectomy arm only)

4.2.3 Safety endpoints Safety endpoints are:

 Death within 90 days after stroke

 Death or dependency (mRS 4-6)

 SICH at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI) as defined in SITS-MOST

 fatal SICH at 24 (±6) hours (CT or MRI)

 parenchymal hemorrhage type 2 (PH-2) 90 (±14) days after stroke

Final version 3.0

10

[DOCLING_PAGE 196]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

 Frequencies of AEs in both treatment arms within the first 7 days after treatment

 Serious AEs (SAEs), overall (all-cause mortality) and stroke-related death (i.e., related to intracranial bleeding, midline shift, brain herniation, progression of stroke symptoms, or systemic complications from stroke such as pneumonia).

 Space-occupying infarction (malignant brain edema) 90 (±14) days after stroke

 New ischemic stroke 90 (±14) days after stroke

5 STUDY METHODS

5.1 Overall Study Design and Plan

TENSION is an investigator-initiated, prospective, open label, blinded endpoint (PROBE), multinational, European, two-arm, randomized, controlled, post-market clinical trial. Patients will be randomized 1:1 to either best medical care + endovascular thrombectomy or best medical care alone. It is planned to recruit 665 patients in approximately 40 sites in eight to ten European countries, to be followed-up for 12 months.

5.2 Selection of Study Population

Patients eligible for initial study screening are those who present with symptoms attributable to acute thrombotic occlusion in the M1 segment of the MCA and/or distal ICA treatable within 12 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well. All such patients who meet all general eligibility criteria will be randomized

Clinical Inclusion criteria

 Randomization within 11 hours after stroke onset (if known) or last seen well.

 Endovascular treatment is expected to be finished within 12 hours after known symptom onset or last seen well by judgment of the interventional neuroradiologist in charge

Final version 3.0

11

[DOCLING_PAGE 197]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

 Patient must demonstrate clinical signs and symptoms attributable to target area of occlusion consistent with the diagnosis of ischemic stroke, including impairment of the following: language, motor function, sensation, cognition, gaze, and/or vision for at least 30 minutes without relevant improvement.

 Female and male patient above 18 up to 80 years of age

 Moderate to severe stroke (NIHSS Score of <26)

 Signed informed consent (IC) form by patient or legal guardian, or inclusion of patient's presumptive will by investigator under emergency situation, but after consultation of an independent physician who is familiar with these types of illness if the other options are not possible according to local approval.

 Prior to new focal neurological deficit, mRS score was ≤2.

Imaging Inclusion Criteria

 A new focal occlusion confirmed by imaging (MRA/CTA) to be accessible to the thrombectomy device, and located in the M1 of the middle cerebral artery (MCA) and/or the intracranial segment of the distal internal carotid artery (ICA).

 CT (non-contrast CT) or DWI with an ASPECT score of 3-5

Clinical exclusion criteria

 Patient is an active participant in another drug or device treatment trial for any disease state or patient is expected to start participation in another drug or device treatment trial while enrolled in this protocol, unless approved by Sponsor.

 Patient has pre-existing neurological or psychiatric disease that could impede the study results or would confound the neurological or functional evaluations.

 Patient has history of contraindication for contrast medium.

 Patient is known to have infective endocarditis

Final version 3.0

12

[DOCLING_PAGE 198]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan

ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

 Patient's anticipated life expectancy is less than 12 months

Imaging exclusion criteria

 Extracranial CT or MR angiography showing high grade stenosis expected to require acute stent placement (e.g. ICA stenting) during the procedure

 CT or MRI with evidence of mass effect or intracranial tumor

 If CT perfusion is done per institutional standard: CBV drop on CTP with an ASPECT score of 0-2

5.3 Method of Treatment Assignment and Randomization

Randomization will be done via the eCRF. Subjects will be randomized to one of the following treatment arms: 'best medical care' or 'best medical care plus thrombectomy'

A randomization schedule (1:1 ratio, best medical care alone: best medical care plus thrombectomy) stratified by stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 6-11 hours) will be generated as described in Section 8.

To randomize the subject, an authorized member of the study site staff will access the webbased system with their individual account information, and will, at a minimum, input the subject identification number, NIHSS, and time from symptom onset. The site will then be provided with a treatment assignment for the subject. Only one subject may be randomized at a time. Randomization assignments will be documented.

The randomization list will be prepared by the Independent Statistical Centre at the Hospices Civils de Lyon (France). It will be sent only to the Data Coordinating Centre in charge of central randomization and data management.

6 SEQUENCE OF PLANNED ANALYSES

6.1 Interim Analyses

The sponsor has the right to halt or terminate the study in any case of concern for the safety of the patients resulting from new information.

Two sequential interim analyses are planned after data on one third or two thirds of the total number of patients with a 90-day visit have been collected to monitor early efficacy of treatment effect or futility (rejection of the null hypothesis at the final analysis highly unlikely).

The interim analysis pattern to be used will employ a power cumulative error spending function (Jennison, C. and Turnbull, B. W. (2000) Group Sequential Methods with Applications to Clinical Trials, New York: Chapman & Hall), with a parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility). The corresponding stopping boundaries (Z-values) at the 2 interim analyses and the final analysis are equal to 3.11, 2.46 and 2.01 for the alpha spending function (efficacy), and -2.38, -2.11 and 2.01 for the betapending function (futility). The corresponding cumulative stopping probabilities under the null hypothesis (CRef=0) and alternative hypothesis (CRef=1) are given in the table below:

|  | Expected |  | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities | Stopping Probabilities |

[/DOCLING_TABLE]

The test statistic generated from the ordinal logistic model is the t-statistic for the treatment parameter, calculated by dividing the parameter estimate by its standard error (Dements, Land 1994). If the z-statistic based on the current number of patients is crosses the prespecified limits, the DSMB may recommend suspending the trial for early success or futility.

Summary safety reports will be generated and provided to the DSMB for review at prespecified time intervals, at least three times over the course of the study, where the first and second time may not be after the first and second interim analysis, respectively. Details of the DSMB reports and review processes will be defined in the DSMB charter.

These stopping rules should be considered as guidelines. The decision to stop or continue the trial will be based on the overall assessment of risk and benefit.

If the study is stopped early (for efficacy or futility), the estimate given for the treatment effect will be the median unbiased estimate, with bias adjusted one-sided 97.5% confidence limits (Group Sequential Methods with Applications to Clinical Trials. Christopher Jennison and Bruce W. Turnbull, CRC/Chapman & Hall, U.K., 2000). P-values will be calculated using the stagewise ordering approach.

If the study is not stopped early, maximum likelihood estimates will be provided, with the associated repeated 97.5% confidence intervals.

Final version 3.0

15

[DOCLING_PAGE 201]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

During the time between database lock and first interim analysis, patients were still included into the study. However, due to external data, it was decided to stop the inclusion into the study.

A final analysis of all patients included with 90 days of follow-up is planned.

Depending on the results of the first interim analysis, two situations are considered:

-the results crossed a boundary at the first interim analysis; the final results will contain

o the results for the primary endpoint at the interim analysis (with test according to spending rules defined above)

o the results for the primary endpoint for all included patients (without test, only confidence intervals)

o results for all other endpoints, populations, and subgroup analyses based on all included patients

-the results did not cross a boundary at the first interim analysis; in this case, the final analysis will be considered as a second interim analysis

6.2 Final Analyses and Reporting

All final, planned, analyses identified in the protocol and in this SAP will be performed only after the formal database lock. A blinded data review meeting will be held prior to database lock and completion of the final analyses. In addition, no database may be locked, random code unblinded, or analyses completed until this SAP has been approved.

Key statistics and study results will be made available to the Steering Committee following database lock and prior to completion of the final CSR.

7 SAMPLE SIZE DETERMINATION

Simulations were carried out using an assumption for the possible true distribution of mRS from the literature (Goyal et al 2015, Supplement: distribution of mRS in patients with an ASPECT score of 7 or less (5)) and a proportional odds alternative with an odds ratio of 1.5 to be assessed using the primary endpoint mRS shift analysis. Outcome profiles were assumed for the treatment arm, as in Table 2 (for each row, table entries are the assumed proportion of subjects in the patient population who would have the indicated mRS outcome if treated with the control or thrombectomy, respectively).

[DOCLING_TABLE rows=3 columns=7]
|  | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5&6 |
| Control | 0.02 | 0.06 | 0.12 | 0.16 | 0.24 | 0.40 |
| Thrombectomy | 0.03 | 0.09 | 0.16 | 0.18 | 0.23 | 0.31 |
[/DOCLING_TABLE]

mRS Outcome

Under the assumption of these distributions, a total of 620 patients is required to achieve a power of 80% for a one-sided test at the 0.025 level.

The trial will undergo two interim analyses with possible premature stopping for futility or early success. As a consequence, the total sample size needs to be increased. With a power cumulative error spending function of parameter ρ=3 (i.e. similar to the O'Brien and Fleming sequential plan) for the alpha-spending function (efficacy), and a parameter equal to 8 for the beta-spending function (futility), up to a maximum of 633 patients will be required if the trial does not stop early.

Final version 3.0

17

[DOCLING_PAGE 203]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

Assuming a 5% drop-out rate of patients to assess the primary endpoint obtained 3 months after inclusion an effective sample size of 665 should be planned to be recruited.

8 ANALYSIS POPULATIONS

The following analysis populations are planned for the studies:

Full Analysis Set (EFFICACY): [or Intent-to-Treat] The Efficacy Population includes all patients who were randomized. Patients will be analyzed in the treatment group to which they were allocated by randomization.

Per-Protocol Efficacy (PP-EFFICACY): The PP-Efficacy includes all patients randomized to a study treatment who received the study treatment according to the treatment protocol and who are judged to comply with all clinical and imaging inclusion and exclusion criteria according to the results of central image reading. Patients who were randomized by the local investigators but are judged to violate imaging inclusion or exclusion criteria by the Central Image Reading Board (CIRB) will be excluded from the randomized patients to create the PPEfficacy population.

A list of patients to be excluded from the randomized patients to create the PP-Efficacy analysis will be established and validated by the Steering Committee prior to unblinded analysis.

Safety Population (SAFETY): The Safety Population includes all patients who received any amount of a planned study intervention, e.g., in whom thrombectomy was initiated and groin puncture was attempted. In case of violation of the randomization scheme, patients will be classified according to the treatment they actually received. A listing of patients that should be switched from one arm to the other, of excluded from the safety analysis, will be established and validated by the Steering Committee prior to unblinded analysis.

Final version 3.0

18

[DOCLING_PAGE 204]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

Patients will be assigned to the different populations prior to unblinded final analysis.

9 GENERAL ISSUES FOR STATISTICAL ANALYSIS

9.1 Analysis Software

All statistical analyses will be performed using SAS ® Software version 9.4 or later in a Windows environment.

9.2 Methods for Withdrawals, Missing Data, and Outliers

All efforts will be made to collect outcome data also in patients withdrawn from the trial for whichever reasons and to minimize the amount of missing data.

9.2.1 Withdrawals

All patients with consent withdrawal will be excluded from the efficacy analysis.

9.2.2 Missing data

Sensitivity analyses based on different hypotheses about the missingness pattern of the primary outcome will be performed to test for the robustness of the primary analysis (see section 'Primary Efficacy Variable Analysis').

9.3 Data Transformations

Analysis of continuous variables may require transformation to normalize the distributions, but these variables will be primarily analyzed using non parametric statistical tests. Decisions to transform the distribution of some variables for analysis may be taken after the blind review of the data, and before unblinding the treatment code.

Final version 3.0

19

[DOCLING_PAGE 205]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

9.4 Multicenter Studies

No adjustment on centre will be performed for the primary analysis, on the basis of the following rationale:

The TENSION trial is aimed to recruit 660 patients from about 40 centres in 8 to 10 european countries, hence an average of 16 patients per centre. However, it is likely that many centres will recruit far fewer patients, while a small number of big centres will recruit much more. Such a situation is an argument for not adjusting the primary analysis on centres. As ICH9 guideline quotes:

'In some trials, for example some large mortality trials with very few subjects per centre, there may be no reason to expect the centres to have any influence on the primary or secondary variables because they are unlikely to represent influences of clinical importance.'

In addition, the protocol did not plan to randomize patients with stratification on centres.

9.5 Multiple Comparisons and Multiplicity

Multiple testing because of repeated sequential analyses on the main efficacy and safety criteria is accounted for to adjust the type-one error rate (see section 5.1).

No adjustment for multiple testing will be made to control the type-one error rate on the other statistical tests. However, the interpretation of any significant test should account for the inflated risk of false positive results due to multiple testing.

9.6 Planned Subgroups, Interactions, and Covariates

Subgroup analyses will be performed on the two stratification variables, i.e. stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (< 6h and 611 hours), and a few other important patient characteristics (see section 13.3 Exploratory analyses for details) .

Final version 3.0

20

[DOCLING_PAGE 206]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon Confidential

These analyses are exploratory and will not lead to formal inferential conclusions about the treatment effect in any particular subgroup.

9.7 Derived and Computed Variables

Some derived and computed variables have been initially identified, and described as appropriate later in the document. It is expected that additional variables will be required. The SAP will not be amended for additional variables that are not related to the primary target or key secondary target variables. Any additional derived or computed variables will be identified and documented in the SAS programs that create analysis files.

9.8 Presentation of results

Description of continuous variables includes the mean, standard deviation, median, 1st and 3rd quartile, and range.

Description of categorical variables includes the frequency and percentage.

Description of ordered variables includes the frequency, percentage and cumulative percentage.

Unless for the primary endpoint, two-sided 95% confidence intervals will be presented, and p-value ≤ 5 % will be considered significant.

For the primary endpoint, one-sided 97.5% confidence intervals will be presented.

Final version 3.0

21

[DOCLING_PAGE 207]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan

ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

9.9 Statistical tests

Unless otherwise specified, between-group differences will be tested using the Wilcoxon rank sum test for continuous outcomes, using the Fisher exact test for binary outcomes, and using the Cochran-Armitage trend test for ordinal outcomes.

13 EFFICACY ANALYSES

Statistical analyses of the primary and secondary efficacy endpoints will primarily be based on the Efficacy (Intent-to-treat) population. Secondarily, the same analyses will be repeated on the Per-protocol population.

All statistical tests will be two-sided tests.

The variables in the source database to be used in the analyses are identified in the text as 'DATASET.VARIABLENAME'

13.1 Primary effectiveness variable Analysis

13.1.1 Formal analysis

The primary effectiveness endpoint for a subject is the blinded evaluation of the ordinal mRS outcome at 90 (+/-14) days post-stroke ( MRS.QSORRES, SVSEQ=7).

The primary effectiveness endpoint analysis (referred to as a 'mRS shift' analysis) is carried out as a parameter test in a proportional-odds logistic regression model with the mRS ordinal scale as response variable and treatment group, stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) ( IC.STRAT1) , time from symptom onset until randomization (0-6 hours and 611hours)( IC.STRAT2 ) as explanatory variables. For this mRS shift analysis, ordinal mRS outcomes in categories 0 and 1 are pooled into one category. If the model does not converge, categories 0, 1 and 2 are pooled into one category. The null and alternative hypotheses for this primary endpoint are H0: β=0 and HA: β≠0, where β is the parameter for the treatment effect in the logistic model. This model is based on the assumption of proportional odds, with constant underlying treatment effect (odds ratio) at any level of the MRS scale (Agresti, A. (2002), Categorical Data Analysis, Second Edition, New York: John Wiley & Sons). If the assumption of proportional odds is not fulfilled for any variable of the model (treatment group or stratification variables), different sets of coefficients will be included in the model to describe the relationship between each pair of outcome groups for the variables for which the hypothesis is not fulfilled. The treatment effect will be tested using the likelihood ratio test of the treatment parameter. The Wald confidence interval of the treatment effect will be provided, with coverage probability in accordance with the nominal alpha level of the final analysis .

Final version 3.0

26

[DOCLING_PAGE 212]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

In a secondary analysis, the primary analysis will be repeated for the per protocol population.

All MRS data evaluated at the 90-day visit ( SVSEQ=7 ) will be used in the analysis, whatever the actual date of assessment. Missing MRS scores in patients who died before day 90 will be assigned a score of 6.

Missing primary outcome values will be replaced using multiple imputation technics. Multiple imputations will be performed using PROC MI in SAS. Fully conditional specification (FCS) method will be used in this SAS procedure. Missing baseline NIHSS value ( NIHSS.NIHSS_TOTAL, SVSEQ=1 ) will be imputed based on a linear model including baseline age ( DM.AGE ). Missing NIHSS value measured at hospital discharge ( NIHSS.NIHSS_TOTAL, SVSEQ=6) will be imputed based on a linear model including baseline age, baseline NIHSS and study group. Missing MRS values at 90 days will be imputed based on a logistic regression model including baseline age, baseline NIHSS, study group and NIHSS at hospital discharge). Imputed values will be rounded to the closest valid mRS and NHISS scores. Minimum and maximum values for NHISS imputed scores will be set at 0 and 42. A total of 5 multiply imputed datasets will be generated to get the final treatment effect estimate and associated confidence interval using PROC MIANALYZE.

Final version 3.0

[DOCLING_PAGE 213]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan

ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

13.1.2 Sensitivity and complementary analyses

A comparison of the baseline characteristics will be performed to assess any imbalance between patients with or without missing primary endpoint.

The reason for missing primary endpoint will be described by treatment group.

In order to assess the influence of the missing primary endpoints on the treatment effect estimate, sensitivity analyses will be performed using the same statistical methods as for the formal analysis, considering two opposite situations:

1/ Worst-case scenario:

All patients with a missing primary endpoint will be considered in the highest category (MRS=6) in the active treatment group, and in the lowest category (MRS=0-1 (or 0-1-2 if the model does not converged)) in the control group (the most unfavorable situation).

2/ Best-case scenario:

All patients with a missing primary endpoint will be considered in the lowest category (MRS=0-1 (or 0-1-2 if the model does not converged)) in the active treatment group, and in the highest category (MRS=6) in the control group (the most favorable situation).

In a complementary analysis, an ordinal logistic regression model will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, retaining the randomization stratification factors, the center effect entered as a random effect (possibly merging some small centers), as well as all clinical and imaging candidate baseline variables associated with the outcome with p-value <0.10.



Overall survival

The follow-up will be censored so that the number of patients at risk still followed at the end of the follow-up is sufficient to estimate survival. A proportional hazard regression model l with Efron's method of handling ties will be fitted to estimate the hazard ratio associated with treatment effect over the total follow-up time, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

Survival probabilities will be plotted using Kaplan-Meier survival estimates in each treatment group.

13.3 Exploratory analyses

Subgroup analyses will be performed on the two stratification variables (IC.STRAT1, IC.STRAT2) , i.e. stroke severity (NIHSS 8-18 and NIHSS >18) and time from symptom onset until randomization (0-6 hours and 6-11hours).

For each of these variables, a proportional-odds logistic regression model will be fitted, relating the log-odds of the primary outcome with the covariate of interest, its interaction

These analyses are exploratory in nature and no definitive conclusion on the treatment effect in one or the other subgroups will be drawn.

14 SAFETY AND TOLERABILITY ANALYSES

Safety Analysis

Safety analyses will be performed on the Safety population only.

14.1.1 Safety endpoints analysis

The following safety endpoints will be studied:

 Death or dependency 90 (±10) days after stroke (MRS 4-6)

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

Patients with missing outcome will not be included in the regression analysis.

 Mortality rate at discharge and 90 days post-treatment

The survival at day 90 will be described according to the Kaplan-Meier estimates in the two groups. A proportional hazard regression model l with Efron's method of handling ties will be fitted to estimate the hazard ratio associated with treatment effect during the first 90 days (events after 90 days or follow-up after 90 days will be censored at day 90), restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

 Stroke-related death

Correspond to patients died (AE.AESEV='5-Death') with cause of death related to disease (AE.AERELDEV='Related (reasonable possibility))

An unconditional logistic regression model (Breslow and Day, 1980) will be fitted to estimate the odds-ratio associated with treatment effect, restricting the adjustment for the randomization stratified factors stroke severity and time from symptom onset until randomization. Corresponding confidence intervals will be provided.

If data are not sufficient for logistic regression, the percentages will be calculated, and compared using the Fisher test.

14.2 Tolerability Analysis (adverse events)

Tolerability analyses will be performed on the Safety population only.

14.2.1 Adverse Events

Adverse events will be coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) central coding dictionary.

14.2.1.1 All Adverse Events

 Any adverse event during the first 7 days following randomization

Proportions will be compared between treatment groups using the Fisher exact test.

Summaries of incidence rates (frequencies and percentages), intensity, and relationship to study drug of individual AEs by System Organ Class and Preferred Term (MedDRA) will be prepared. Each patient will be counted only once within each category (System Organ Class or Preferred Term). If a patient experiences more than one AE within a category, only the AE with the strongest relationship or the greatest intensity, as appropriate, will be included in the summaries of relationship and intensity.

Final version 3.0

35

[DOCLING_PAGE 221]

TENSION - Statistical Analysis Plan ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT03094715

Hospices Civils de Lyon

Confidential

Summaries will be presented by treatment group for all patients in the Safety Population. Fisher's exact test will be used to compare proportions for System Organ Classes across treatment groups.

Counts of AEs by maximum intensity will be presented for patients in the Safety Population by treatment group. Only AEs beginning at or after the beginning of study drug administration will be included. Similarly, counts of AEs by strongest relationship to study drug will be presented.

A data listing of AEs leading to withdrawal will also be provided, displaying details of the event(s) captured on the CRF.

14.2.1.2 Serious Adverse Events

Serious adverse event reconciliation will be performed by Data Management, Clinical Research, and Pharmacovigilance via data listings.

SAEs occurring up to the 90-day visit will be described

A summary of incidence rates (frequencies and percentages) of SAEs by treatment group, System Organ Class, and Preferred Term will be prepared for the Safety Population. No statistical tests will be performed.

A data listing of SAEs will also be provided, displaying details of the event(s) captured on the CRF.
